# 301: The "Most Dashing" Duel!

Tinkaton's spectacular display of spinning and smashing Steelix left the audience in awe. It was the very definition of "brutal elegance."

People had seen hammers swung, rocks thrown, but tossing a Steelix like a toy? That was a first.

With this triumph, Johan received the Luck Symbol from Lucy, marking his conquest of the Battle Pike.

From this day forth, who would dare claim Johan wasn't a luck god?

Lucy, as always, didn't have much to say. She simply offered her congratulations and left the stage quickly.

But no one's feelings about the match were as mixed as Scott's.

He had braced himself for some arena damage — that was par for the course with Johan's battles.

What he hadn't prepared for was the utter destruction of the Battle Pike as a facility.

Cracked walls, a ceiling on the brink of collapse, and a floor that looked like it had survived an earthquake.

Only now did Scott truly understand the saying: "Reputation is earned."

Johan's reputation as the "Battlefield Wrecker" wasn't just talk.

Still, the surge in popularity was undeniable. The livestream had brought countless eyes to the Battle Frontier.

As Tinkaton strutted out of the Battle Pike with its hammer slung over its shoulder, proudly wearing the Luck Symbol, the audience erupted into cheers.

Night had fallen by the time Johan and his group left.

Rather than checking into a hotel, Steven invited the group to stay at a luxury resort owned by the Devon Corporation in the Battle Frontier.

It wasn't a typical resort. It featured beautifully preserved natural environments alongside spacious, standalone wooden cabins.

The setup allowed guests to enjoy the outdoors while still sleeping comfortably indoors.

As night fell, Johan, his Pokémon, and the others gathered around a campfire outside their cabin.

The warm glow of the fire painted their faces orange as they chatted beneath the glimmering night sky.

"You've been holding out on us, Steven," Flannery grumbled playfully. "This place is amazing."

Steven rubbed the back of his head sheepishly.

"I only found out about this resort today. My father saw Johan's stream earlier and mentioned it."

"Your dad watches the stream?" Roxanne asked, raising an eyebrow.

Steven nodded with a grin.

"He's a big fan of Johan's. He even sent fifty Dragonite gifts in the chat earlier."

Johan winced.

Fifty Dragonite gifts?!

Why not just transfer that money to him directly? After all, the livestream earnings went to Scott, not him.

Flannery chuckled to herself.

Maybe I should convince Grandpa to send a few gifts too… That way, Johan won't "accidentally" destroy Lavaridge Gym when he visits.

Johan, meanwhile, was busy preparing snacks over the campfire.

With Flannery's Torkoal providing the perfect level of heat, the Pokémon gathered eagerly around him.

Pokémon-style teatime was in full swing.

The sound of sizzling food filled the air.

"Alright, it's ready!"

Johan began handing out snacks to his team, making sure each Pokémon got their share.

[Johan, delicious! Johan, delicious!]

Jirachi floated in circles, its ribbons twirling with joy as it munched on its treat.

Even Torkoal, who was usually quite stoic, let out a contented hum when Johan handed it a special portion.

Seeing her Torkoal so happy, Flannery sighed.

"Johan, you're so good to every Pokémon. No wonder they all adore you."

"It's not just Pokémon," Steven chimed in with a smile. "People admire him too."

After dinner, Johan leaned back against Alolan Ninetales, gazing at the starry sky.

The Pokémon played together in the yard.

"Higher! Throw me higher!"

Jirachi's giggles filled the air as Tinkaton gleefully tossed it higher and higher into the sky, its hammer lying momentarily forgotten on the ground.

Meanwhile, Gengar was off in the corner, sipping tea brewed with Energy Roots and regaling the other Pokémon with exaggerated stories.

"Ka-kah…"

Back when the world was young… I, the great Gengar…

It had a natural flair for storytelling, and even the most serious Pokémon, like Metagross and Aggron, were drawn in.

The night was filled with laughter, warmth, and camaraderie, echoing through the resort as the group rested under the ancient comet's glow.

On the third day of the comet's appearance, Johan awoke with a start.

A suffocating sensation had jolted him awake.

When he reached up, he realized Jirachi's ribbon had somehow ended up draped over his face.

Taking a deep breath, he chuckled softly.

"Phew… For a second, I thought a Snorlax had rolled onto me."

Looking up, he noticed Gengar snoozing contentedly on the ceiling.

Guess it slept well, he thought, nodding in approval.

By the time he got up, the Pokémon were already raring to go.

[Johan, what are we playing today?]

Jirachi, who had been unusually well-behaved last night, hovered around him excitedly.

"We've still got a lot of facilities left to clear," Johan mused as he sipped his coffee.

Yesterday, he had conquered the Battle Tower and the Battle Pike.

Now, only the Battle Palace, Arena, Dome, Factory, and Pyramid remained.

"Not the Pyramid," he muttered to himself.

With Brandon guarding it, the Pyramid was the one facility he had no confidence in clearing.

The others, however, were fair game.

After some deliberation, Johan made his decision.

"We'll tackle the Battle Dome today."

The announcement made Jirachi spin with joy, its ribbons trailing in the air.

[Yay! Battle Dome! Let's go!]

The Most Dashing Duel

News spread quickly.

By the time Johan arrived at the Battle Dome, Scott was already there, accompanied by a full filming crew.

[Johan's Guide to the Battle Frontier] was live once again.

"Another early stream, huh?"

"Good morning, Johan!"

"Late start today? You're making up for it by clearing two facilities, right?"

Johan chuckled at the teasing comments in the chat.

"Morning, everyone," he greeted casually.

"Morning?! Pay your fines for being late!"

"Is that… the Battle Dome?!"

"Omg, I'm here in person! Johan looks even more handsome in real life!"

"How much for a selfie, mate? Let's split the profit!"

Johan couldn't help but laugh. Viewers will be viewers, no matter the world.

Scott, as expected, had outdone himself.

The Battle Dome was packed to capacity, with fans eager to watch Johan's next challenge.

Inside the opulent arena, cheers erupted as Johan stepped onto the battlefield.

He could feel the energy in the air — most of these people weren't here for the Battle Dome.

They were here for him.

Across the battlefield, a temporary stage had been set up.

Standing atop it was the Dome Ace, Tucker, in all his flamboyant glory.

Dressed in a dazzling purple outfit with elaborate twin ponytails, Tucker twirled dramatically, soaking in the crowd's adoration.

"Listen to those cheers, Johan!" Tucker called out, his voice dripping with theatrics.

"Today, we're about to witness the ultimate showdown: the duel between the most dashing trainers in the Pokémon world!"

# 302: Burn It All, Charcadet!

Tucker leaped down from the elevated stage, with spotlights illuminating his every move.

"Welcome, my beautiful audience, to the Battle Dome!"

Cheers erupted from the crowd.

"Tucker!"

Without prompting, Johan's Poké Ball opened on its own, and Kubfu stood in front of him, its expression serious and fists flying.

"Ku~!"

"Foul fiend!"

Johan facepalmed, recalling Kubfu.

"It's not your turn yet."

Amid the roaring applause, Tucker landed on the battlefield, arms outstretched, and exclaimed with unabashed self-admiration:

"Tonight, my friends, I invite you all to bask in the beauty of battle!"

The audience responded with even louder cheers.

Much of the cheering came from middle-aged women, to Johan's amusement.

Sitting in the audience, Flannery and the others exchanged surprised glances.

"Wait... so not everyone's here just to see Johan battle?"

"Of course not," Steven replied. "Tucker is a popular star in his own right."

Before Johan arrived, the Battle Frontier's revenue depended heavily on Tucker's popularity. This was why Scott was confident he could pack the arena overnight—Tucker had his own fanbase.

"I just don't get it," Roxanne muttered, blushing slightly at Tucker's flamboyant movements.

Even Steven and Flannery couldn't help but nod in agreement.

Meanwhile, Jirachi's eyes sparkled with excitement as it floated around Johan.

"Johan, I'm ready!"

Tucker struck another exaggerated pose, signaling the start of the battle.

"Now, prepare for tonight's main event—a fusion of fire and water!"

With a flourish, he released two Poké Balls.

Out came Swampert and Arcanine.

"Swampert, the Mud Fish Pokémon, a Water/Ground type and the final evolution of Mudkip!"

"Arcanine, the Legendary Pokémon, a Fire type and the evolved form of Growlithe!"

Johan recognized this duo as Tucker's signature combination. His infamous "Fusion of Fire and Water" strategy was on full display.

Johan avoided meeting Tucker's gaze. Something about Tucker's over-the-top behavior made him secondhand embarrassed.

Instead, he focused on countering.

"Swampert and Arcanine, huh? Water and fire..." Johan smirked as he tossed two Poké Balls of his own.

Out came Dragonair and Charcadet!

While Swampert and Arcanine had excellent synergy, Johan opted to rely on pure firepower and teamwork, confident in his partners' abilities.

Charcadet was making its formal battle debut, and Johan had high hopes.

As for Dragonair, it was the picture of reliability.

"Let the battle... begin!"

"Arcanine!"

With a thunderous roar, Arcanine unleashed its Intimidate ability, lowering Charcadet and Dragonair's attack power.

The referee's signal echoed across the arena, officially starting the fight.

"Dragon Dance, Dragonair!" Johan called out.

"Draaa!"

Dragonair's delicate wings extended, shimmering as it gracefully spun through the air, increasing its attack and speed.

Tucker, however, wasted no time.

"Arcanine, Extreme Speed! Swampert, Earthquake!"

Arcanine became a blur of motion, targeting Dragonair, while Swampert slammed its fists into the ground, triggering a massive tremor aimed at Charcadet.

"Charcadet, grab Dragonair's tail!"

Johan's tone was calm, but his body was tense.

Unbeknownst to the others, Charcadet's fiery determination was resonating with Johan's aura, amplifying his connection to the battle.

Charcadet followed his command without hesitation, latching onto Dragonair's tail as the serpent Pokémon deftly avoided the tremors.

Its fiery crest glowed brighter, its flames responding to its own growing excitement.

"Flamethrower, Charcadet!"

With a deep breath, Charcadet unleashed a powerful stream of flames, aimed at the oncoming Arcanine.

BOOM!

The fire collided midair with Arcanine's charge, forcing the Legendary Pokémon to retreat momentarily.

"Such intensity! That's no ordinary Flamethrower!"

"Johan's Charcadet must be running on pure adrenaline!"

"Who needs strategy when you can have this much firepower?"

The battle raged on. Swampert attempted to support with Water Pulse, while Arcanine launched a ferocious Flare Blitz, each attack clashing against Dragonair and Charcadet's coordinated counters.

Eventually, both sides paused, eyeing each other across the battlefield.

Tucker smirked.

"Arcanine, Overheat! Swampert, Hydro Pump!"

"This is it! Fire and water, united!"

Johan met Tucker's enthusiasm with a grin of his own.

"Dragonair, Helping Hand! Charcadet, Overheat!"

Dragonair glowed, channeling energy into Charcadet.

"Goka... GOKA!"

Charcadet's flames roared to life, engulfing its body as it braced for its strongest attack yet.

Both sides unleashed their attacks simultaneously.

The arena was bathed in blinding light as fire and water collided, creating a superheated steam explosion that enveloped the battlefield.

When the dust settled, Swampert and Arcanine lay unconscious amidst the smoldering remains of the arena.

Charcadet stood victorious, its charred body still radiating faint embers.

The audience erupted into applause.

"Burn it all, Charcadet!"

"That was a battle for the ages!"

Tucker, though scorched and disheveled, laughed heartily.

"Well done, Johan! Take this Tactics Symbol as proof of your victory!"

The battle ended with both trainers exchanging a nod of respect, as the crowd roared in approval.

# 303: Kubfu, Show Them Something Stunning!

Sinnoh Region

"Whoa! That Charcadet's flames are incredible!"

Riley reclined casually on the couch, one arm draped over the backrest, his expression alight with excitement.

He'd heard plenty about Johan. Especially from Cynthia and Volkner during their conversations, where Johan's battles were a frequent topic of discussion. Riley had even caught some of Johan's performances during the Indigo League broadcasts.

"Man, when's he coming to Sinnoh? I'd love to have a battle with him!"

Riley smacked his lips, speaking as if to himself.

Charcadet's fiery display had ignited his enthusiasm.

He envisioned a fierce clash with Johan—one where their battle would serve as a test of resolve and strength, full of heart-pounding action.

From a single-seater sofa nearby, Lucian calmly closed his book, adjusted his glasses, and threw a discreet glance toward Cynthia, seated at the other end of the couch.

Riley, you really don't read the room, do you?

Haven't you noticed how Cynthia's smile deepens every time you mention challenging Johan?

But Riley had always been laser-focused on battles, oblivious to trivial matters like that.

What he also hadn't realized was that Johan wasn't just any trainer—he was a mentor to an extraordinary student and had strong ties with Kanto's Lance, Lorelei, and Cynthia herself.

"Alright, I've decided!"

Riley sprang to his feet. "I'm heading to our Battle Frontier! Let's see who can clear it faster—Johan or me!"

Hands in his pockets, he laughed with unmistakable self-satisfaction at his self-declared competition with Johan.

"Roarrr!"

Cynthia's Garchomp snorted twin puffs of white air, giving Riley a disdainful side-eye.

Hey, afro guy—why not spar with me first?

Though Riley couldn't understand Garchomp's growling words, he'd fought Cynthia often enough to recognize that challenging look in her Pokémon's eyes.

His own eyes lit up.

"Garchomp, are you offering to help me train?"

Lucian sighed and rubbed his forehead.

You can't read Cynthia's expressions; fine. But even Garchomp's simmering rage and vengeful glare go right over your head?

Garchomp grinned, its teeth gleaming dangerously.

"Rooarr!"

It nodded firmly.

Perfect. Watching Johan's speed runs through the Battle Frontier had left it itching for action, and now Riley had practically volunteered to be its personal punching bag.

What a great guy.

"Awesome! Hahaha!" Riley burst into laughter.

"Once I've cleared the Battle Frontier, I'll challenge the Elite Four! It's my turn to claim the title!"

He marched outside with a swagger, radiating overconfidence.

Ten minutes later.

Aaron strolled past the garden outside and spotted Riley hanging from a tree. Scratching his head, he asked, "Hey, Riley, practicing tree climbing again?"

Hoenn Region, Battle Frontier.

After lunch, Charcadet gleefully accepted a cup of energy root tea from Kubfu. Following Kubfu's example, it sipped delicately.

Bitterness exploded in its mouth.

Charcadet scrunched its face in a comical grimace.

"Kuu~"

Kubfu patted Charcadet's shoulder in an elder sibling-like gesture, taking another sip.

"You'll get used to it," it seemed to say.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar, lounging nearby, quipped, "You don't get used to it—it just numbs your taste buds after a while."

Hearing this, Charcadet shrank back.

Then, an idea struck. Its eyes lit up as it fished out a lump of charcoal. Taking a hearty bite, it crunched off some bits before sipping the tea again.

Its face relaxed into a look of satisfaction.

"Goka!"

Yep, that hit the spot.

Johan couldn't help but marvel.

Using charcoal as a side dish for energy root tea? Only Charcadet could come up with that.

Meanwhile, Ninetales, Tinkaton, and Dragonair gathered at a table, happily munching on treats and energy cubes.

Energy root tea? Not a chance.

Flannery, watching the scene unfold, rubbed her eyes in disbelief.

"Teacher... did you seriously have me buy charcoal as food for Charcadet?"

"Why not?"

Flannery was at a loss for words, question marks practically floating above her head.

Charcoal was supposed to boost Fire-type moves, not be eaten!

"When did charcoal become a snack?"

Johan gave a sheepish laugh.

"Well, it's a Fire-type Pokémon, so isn't eating charcoal reasonable?"

Charcadet, essentially being a living chunk of charcoal itself, seemed to think so.

That afternoon, the group headed to the Battle Arena, the fourth facility in Johan's Battle Frontier challenge.

The arena resembled a traditional dojo, designed to test both trainers and their Pokémon. Victors were awarded the Guts Symbol.

The Battle Arena emphasized three attributes: heart, skill, and body—continuous attacks, critical hits, and remaining stamina, respectively. Points, rather than knockouts, determined the winner.

Kubfu, who entered the arena with Johan, gazed at the stone-paved courtyard where rows of trainees practiced their punches.

The sight reminded it of Master Mustard's dojo back on the Isle of Armor.

Kubfu clenched its fists.

It had grown and improved so much since leaving the Isle. It wondered if the clever, sweet-talking wild Pokémon back there missed it.

"Johan!"

As soon as they stepped inside, the Battle Arena's leader, Greta, came rushing out to greet them.

Her small fists clenched in excitement, and she bowed deeply.

"Teacher Johan! It's an honor to finally meet you!"

Johan was taken aback.

"Wait, what? Teacher?"

Greta stood straight, her expression both eager and reverent.

"I've been waiting for this chance to learn from you!"

Johan realized that Greta, like many others, must have been inspired by his reputation.

Still, wasn't this supposed to be a challenge?

"I'm here to battle, not to teach," Johan clarified.

"Oh!" Greta nodded but called out to her students, "Everyone, clear the courtyard! Teacher Johan is here to demonstrate his techniques!"

"...What?"

Johan sighed. He'd somehow gained a reputation as a martial arts mentor among trainers.

"Oh well," he thought, signaling to Kubfu.

"Let's give them a little shock, Kubfu."

"Ku~~!"

Kubfu stepped forward, its determination shining through.

It was ready to show them all what a true master-in-training could do.

# 304: Kubfu: Watch Closely, I’m Only Showing This Once!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

Kubfu vs. Heracross!

Heracross, renowned as one of the strongest Bug-type Pokémon, could easily rank in the top three for raw power among its kind.

While victory wasn't the sole determinant of success in the Battle Arena—as high enough scores in the other two categories could still lead to passing the trial—losing yet passing could give off the wrong impression.

And with so many people watching, winning decisively was the better way to dispel any doubts about fairness.

This battle wasn't just about winning; it was also about showcasing skill.

As the two trainers and their Pokémon prepared themselves, the apprentice referee raised their voice.

"Battle—begin!"

The referee's announcement had barely echoed when:

"Heracross, Aerial Ace!"

"Kubfu, Aerial Ace!"

Both trainers called out at almost the same time, their commands overlapping. For a brief moment, they paused, exchanged glances, and smiled knowingly at each other.

In the Battle Arena, one of the scoring criteria, "Technique," awarded higher points for moves that hit critically or were super effective. Even a successful hit, even without a critical, would score some points.

For Kubfu, a Fighting-type weak to Flying moves, and Heracross, doubly weak to Flying, Aerial Ace—known for its guaranteed accuracy—was the ideal choice.

Heracross's back carapace opened, revealing gossamer wings that buzzed as it launched itself forward, its horn cutting through the air.

At the same time, Kubfu tightened its headband-like fur and leaped forward, meeting Heracross head-on.

Boom!

Heracross's horn collided with Kubfu's fist, and the impact sent both Pokémon skidding back.

"This feels more like a battle between Flying-types than Fighting-types!"

"Who doesn't know Aerial Ace at this point?"

"Is this some kind of martial artist telepathy?"

For both Pokémon, this initial clash was more of a test, a way to gauge their opponent's strength. The true heart and skill of the battle would come next!

"Heracross, Close Combat!"

Greta, the Battle Arena leader, called out with a commanding gesture.

Heracross buzzed its wings again, its eyes gleaming with determination. Its red-tinged horn surged forward, aiming straight for Kubfu's chest.

But Kubfu, unfazed, sidestepped nimbly, letting Heracross's horn miss by a narrow margin.

As Heracross stumbled slightly, Kubfu crouched and aimed a sharp strike at Heracross's elbow joint.

The moment Kubfu's fist landed, it sensed Heracross subtly adjust its stance, absorbing the blow and retreating just enough to prepare its next move.

This Heracross... isn't bad at all, Kubfu thought, a hint of surprise flickering in its eyes.

Heracross, too, was caught off guard.

This little bear... its fighting skills are extraordinary!

Heracross retaliated by swinging its head downward, aiming to slam Kubfu with its horn, while simultaneously bringing its left arm upward like a hammer.

Kubfu, undeterred, shifted its stance. Twisting its body sideways, it countered with a shoulder block aimed at Heracross's right arm joint.

Heracross, sensing danger, rolled to the left and attempted a sweeping kick.

But Kubfu, anticipating the move, performed a backflip to dodge.

Greta watched the exchange with wide eyes.

These moves... Heracross's Close Combat was completely neutralized by Kubfu!

Though the sound of impacts filled the air, Greta could tell none of Heracross's strikes had truly landed.

So this is one of Johan's Pokémon... No wonder he's known as a martial arts master!

The Battle Arena trainees were equally stunned.

Heracross and Kubfu's clash of techniques was an exhilarating spectacle.

"This little bear just nullified Heracross's Close Combat?!"

"I can't follow everything they're doing, but it's incredible to watch!"

"This is peak martial artistry!"

If Tinkaton embodied raw power and Charcadet represented fiery passion, then Kubfu showcased the pinnacle of combat technique.

Kubfu stood confidently, its face calm.

But through their shared aura connection, Johan could sense Kubfu's nerves—though it held its ground, it knew one misstep could mean taking the full brunt of Heracross's Close Combat.

Johan's expression turned slightly amused.

"Ahem. Kubfu says..."

"Kuu! (▼皿▼)σ"

"Watch closely—I'm only showing this once!"

In the next instant, Kubfu leaped forward, its body radiating red energy.

Close Combat!

Compared to Heracross's earlier assault, Kubfu's Close Combat seemed lighter, swifter, and far more precise.

Bam!

Heracross tried to block, but Kubfu anticipated the move. It slid under Heracross with a sudden feint and landed behind it.

Then came a series of seamless attacks:

Sweeping kick.

Hook punch.

Low lunge.

Elbow strike.

Knee strike.

Rolling flip.

Leg lock...

Kubfu's unrelenting onslaught overwhelmed Heracross.

At first, Heracross managed to counter a few moves. But as the attacks grew faster and stronger, it could no longer keep up.

To Heracross, Kubfu's relentless strikes felt like crashing waves—each more powerful than the last.

Wham!

Heracross was flung into the air before crashing to the ground.

With Kubfu's unyielding flurry of strikes, the Heart category score maxed out!

"This... Is this the Art of Rapid Strikes?!" Greta gasped.

Johan's Kubfu had clearly mastered techniques passed down by Mustard, the Isle of Armor's legendary master.

"Heracross, show your determination! Get back up!"

"Herraaa!"

Heracross groaned, shakily pushing itself off the ground.

Greta grinned triumphantly.

"Mega Horn!"

"Herraa!"

Heracross charged forward, its horn glowing with immense power.

"Kuu!"

Kubfu's eyes lit up with excitement. Planting its feet firmly, it spread its arms wide, summoning a powerful aura.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

Focus Energy?

Kubfu clearly wanted to test its mettle in a head-on clash.

"Alright... Kubfu, Focus Punch!"

Both Pokémon gathered their strength—Heracross's horn radiating green energy, Kubfu's fists glowing white-hot.

BOOM!

The collision sent shockwaves rippling through the arena, even knocking over a few of Greta's trainees.

When the dust cleared, Heracross lay unconscious on the ground, and the Critical Hit category was maxed out!

"Heracross is unable to battle! In all categories—Heart, Skill, and Body—Kubfu is the winner!"

Greta was momentarily stunned before bursting into applause.

"That was amazing!"

She rushed over to Johan and Kubfu, her admiration plain to see.

Johan gave a modest smile.

Greta couldn't help but ask, "Can Kubfu combine Rapid Strikes and Single Strike techniques?"

Johan glanced at Kubfu, who stood tall and serious.

"For now... not quite."

Kubfu wasn't yet able to blend both styles while awake. But with enough training, it could master such a feat even without entering its calmest state during sleep.

As Greta handed over the Guts Symbol, she remarked, "Even without its strongest form, Kubfu is incredible!"

With four Battle Frontier symbols now in his possession, Johan couldn't help but reflect:

Am I already strong enough to trounce ordinary Gym Leaders?

If so...

Maybe it's time to set my sights on the Elite Four—or even... a Battle Frontier head as strong as them.

# 305: Johan: A Step-by-Step Guide to Speedrunning the Battle Factory

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

Discussions about the Battle Frontier were still trending online.

Charcadet's Flamethrower that melted the battlefield and Kubfu's display of masterful combat technique were the hottest topics among the fanbase.

Familiar names added fuel to the fire, amplifying the hype even further.

Bruno: "Indeed, Johan is a highly skilled martial artist. If I were to face him, I wouldn't be confident of winning."

Mustard: "Little Johan is amazing! Kubfu has improved so much—makes this old man want a rematch to redeem some dignity, haha!"

Professor Oak: "Judging by the temperature of that Flamethrower, it likely exceeded 2,000 degrees Celsius. Such terrifying passion!"

As Johan scrolled through these comments on his phone that evening, his expression turned both amused and slightly awkward.

"Have you all been bribed by Scott?"

From just these comments, Johan felt as if people might actually believe he could challenge a regional Champion on his own.

In fact, someone had already taken this seriously: Archie's right-hand man, Shelly.

When Shelly saw the statements by Bruno and Mustard, he broke out in a cold sweat.

"Good thing I ran away in time," Shelly thought, relieved.

Who would've thought this seemingly skinny, mild-mannered man was also a top-tier martial artist? A true hidden master.

For Shelly, the realization explained Johan's incredible skill in wild Pokémon battles—people like him probably trained by wrestling Machamp or grappling Steelix daily.

The next day marked the fourth day since Jirachi's awakening and Johan's third day in the Battle Frontier.

This time, he made an unexpected decision:

Split up his team!

He planned to simultaneously challenge the Battle Palace and the Battle Factory.

The Battle Palace had a peculiar rule: trainers could not give commands to their Pokémon during battles. They could only send out three Pokémon and let them fight on their own instincts and capabilities.

"No trainer involvement? What's even the point of battling?"

For Johan, this felt like a lose-lose situation:

Lose, and the Pokémon might lose confidence in their trainer. Win, and the trainer feels irrelevant.

Luckily, Johan was already used to this kind of arrangement. His Pokémon were more than capable of handling battles independently—they often organized their own midnight "missions" to scavenge for food, after all.

He entrusted this mission to his "Ghost-Bear-Hammer" trio: Gengar, Tinkaton, and Kubfu.

"Ka-kaah! (■_■)"

Gengar, wearing a pair of sunglasses, waved its hand dramatically and marched toward the Battle Palace.

Behind it, Tinkaton followed with its oversized hammer slung over its shoulder, its twin pigtails bouncing with each step.

Kubfu, wearing an unusually serious expression, saluted Johan solemnly.

To Kubfu, this was a critical mission—proof of their trainer's trust.

"I shall slay the enemy, claim their symbol, and return victorious!"

Johan's face twitched.

"...Just don't get lost."

Kubfu snapped back to reality, chasing after Gengar and Tinkaton in a hurry.

"Kuu!! ε=ε=┌(>_<)┘"

The three Pokémon soon vanished from sight, walking in step like a small but mighty army.

Watching them leave, Johan sighed deeply.

"I hope this doesn't end in disaster."

"Don't worry, teacher," Steven reassured him.

"Yeah," Flannery chimed in. "Your Pokémon are strong and experienced. They'll be fine!"

Johan gave her a sidelong glance and replied dryly, "I'm not worried about them. I'm worried about the Battle Palace."

Steven and the others exchanged glances.

Fair point.

"Let's hope the Battle Palace survives," Roxanne muttered awkwardly.

After seeing off his "Ghost-Bear-Hammer" trio, Johan turned his attention to the Battle Factory.

The rules here revolved around rental Pokémon. Trainers could borrow three random Pokémon and use them to battle against similarly equipped opponents.

Upon winning, the trainer could swap one of their Pokémon with one from their opponent's team. After achieving a series of victories, they would face the Factory Head, Noland.

The Factory's emphasis on randomness didn't lower its popularity. If anything, the unpredictability made it even more exciting.

Pokémon were randomized. Opponents were randomized. Held items were randomized.

"It's all about strategy... and a little luck."

When Johan arrived at the factory, he noticed something missing.

"Where's Jirachi?"

"Probably went off with Gengar and the others," Steven guessed.

Johan sighed. "Let it play, then."

Inside the Battle Factory, a staff member handed Johan six Poké Balls and a summary sheet.

"Here are your six randomized Pokémon. Please select three for your team."

Johan glanced at the Pokémon lineup and immediately felt like the universe was trolling him:

Magikarp Volbeat Shroomish Raticate Grimer Zigzagoon

The live chat erupted in laughter:

"HAHAHAHA, there's actually a Magikarp!"

"At least it's just one!"

"Johan's luck stat is officially cursed!"

Even Johan's expression faltered.

He strongly suspected that Scott had rigged this as a prank, but with Scott watching from the corner, Johan could only sigh.

Johan reviewed the limited information provided about the Pokémon and soon narrowed down his choices.

"I'll go with these three."

He chose Volbeat, Shroomish, and Zigzagoon.

The chat immediately exploded again:

"Volbeat, Shroomish, and Zigzagoon? What is this, a beginner trainer's team?"

"Johan's about to teach us how to make the best of a bad situation."

However, once the battles began, viewers started noticing something unusual:

"Wait... is that Volbeat's Prankster ability?"

"Volbeat knows Tail Glow?! That's a +3 to Special Attack!"

"And he's Baton Passing it to Shroomish?!"

The crowd was floored. The seemingly weak Shroomish suddenly became a powerhouse, tearing through the opposing team.

When asked how Shroomish could sustain such relentless attacks, Johan casually explained, "Zigzagoon's ability is Pickup."

Zigzagoon kept recovering leftover berries from the battlefield, ensuring the team never ran out of resources.

"HE'S ACTUALLY DOING IT!"

"Shroomish is going on a rampage!"

"This is the most inspiring underdog story I've ever seen."

With his unique team strategy, Johan blazed through the Battle Factory, leaving both his opponents and the audience stunned.

"And that's how you speedrun the Battle Factory," Johan concluded smugly.

The chat was flooded with comments:

"Teach us, sensei!"

"I'm catching these three Pokémon right now!"

# 307: Alolan Ninetales vs. Articuno! (BONUS)

In the Pokémon anime, Thorton is known to have an Articuno. More accurately, it's a wild Articuno he befriended—similar to Johan's relationships with Mew and Jirachi.

However, Johan couldn't be entirely sure whether Thorton had already established a bond with Articuno at this point. Judging by the current situation, it seemed like Thorton and Articuno had indeed built some form of connection.

That said, their bond was nowhere near as strong as the one Thorton had years later, during Ash's travels.

Moreover, this particular Articuno wasn't as powerful as some others Johan had encountered, such as the legendary Articuno, Zapdos, and Moltres from their various regions.

This conclusion was supported by Articuno's move set and the proficiency displayed in its execution—details visible via the Pokédex.

Articuno and Thorton's connection was rooted in an event where the bird was injured while flying between two snowy peaks. Thorton, piloting a plane at the time, rescued it, leading to their friendship.

A legendary Pokémon that could be hurt just flying between mountains wasn't exactly the most formidable specimen.

Thus, Johan had his reasons for referring to it as a "baby Articuno."

"Screeeeeeech—!"

The high-pitched cry grew louder as Articuno swiftly approached the battlefield.

Chat Commentary:

"Wait... Is that...?"

"ARTICUNO?! How is an Articuno here?"

"No doubt about it—it's here to help Johan. He's the Legendary Whisperer, after all!"

"Thorton already lost two Pokémon, and now he's summoning an Articuno? That's cheating!"

"Alright, fine, admit it—Johan really is the Legendary Whisperer."

However, as Articuno descended beside Thorton, radiating an icy, crystalline glow, the chat collectively froze.

Soon, the screen was flooded with question marks.

"Hold up... That Articuno belongs to Thorton?!"

"Wait, so Johan isn't cheating?"

"This is... awkward."

Thorton laughed as he introduced his relationship with Articuno, recounting the tale of how he rescued the Pokémon.

"Haha! This little guy wants to join the fight. You don't mind, do you, Johan?"

Johan's Inner Thoughts:

I do mind.

But he kept his composure. If I say no, would you actually back down? Probably not.

The chat, oblivious to Johan's predicament, continued its relentless ribbing.

"Of course," Johan replied, expressionless.

At his words, Alolan Ninetales rose, emitting a proud howl: "Oowoo!"

If Johan said it was fine, then it was fine. Ninetales didn't care who the opponent was—even if it was a Legendary Pokémon.

The Matchup: Alolan Ninetales vs. Articuno.

This was a battle of Ice-types.

Articuno cried out, flapping its crystalline wings to stir a biting chill. The frigid winds rolled forward, exuding a sense of immense pressure.

"Dazzling Gleam!" Johan commanded.

Ninetales let out a melodic cry as its body shimmered with rainbow-colored light. The radiance intensified and struck Articuno directly.

Boom!

Caught off guard, Articuno wavered in midair, unprepared for such swift and powerful aggression.

Chat Commentary:

"Ninetales came out swinging!"

"Is this even fair? Look at that power—she's insane!"

With Tailwind and Nasty Plot still active, coupled with Snow Warning and Misty Terrain, Ninetales was as powerful as ever.

But Articuno also benefited from the Snow Warning, gaining an added boost to its strength.

"Articuno, Haze!" Thorton commanded.

Articuno flapped its wings, unleashing a swirling black mist that covered the battlefield.

[Haze: Resets all stat changes on the field.]

Thorton had quickly identified the danger posed by Ninetales' boosted Special Attack.

But Johan smirked. "White Mist."

[White Mist: Protects the user's team from having its stats lowered.]

Ninetales flicked its tails gracefully, releasing a frosty mist that clung to its form. The ethereal glow made the elegant fox Pokémon seem almost otherworldly.

The result? Ninetales' stat boosts remained intact.

However, Thorton's plan wasn't entirely foiled—his Haze had also ended Tailwind, removing Johan's team's speed advantage.

"Articuno, set up Tailwind!"

Articuno flapped its wings once more, creating a gusty breeze. The battlefield's tempo shifted, with Articuno now claiming the speed advantage.

In response, Ninetales quietly conjured an Aurora Veil, bolstering its defense.

"Blizzard!" Thorton called.

"Ooowo!"

Snowstorm met snowstorm as Articuno unleashed a freezing gale.

It was the first time Ninetales had ever been on the receiving end of Blizzard.

Johan's eyes narrowed as he met Ninetales' gaze. He didn't need words to understand its thoughts.

This was a matter of pride.

"You've come this far," Johan said softly. "Let's prove it together."

"Blizzard!"

Ninetales roared, releasing a gale of its own.

The two blizzards collided violently, sending waves of frost and snow spiraling throughout the arena. Icy patterns etched themselves into walls, windows, and floors.

Chat Commentary:

"This is nuts! Two Blizzards clashing head-on?"

"Alolan Ninetales is going toe-to-toe with Articuno... and winning?!"

Articuno flapped its wings harder, pouring more power into its Blizzard. But Ninetales stood firm, its determination unwavering.

The collision culminated in a massive explosion of ice and wind.

When the frost cleared, Articuno stumbled midair, its wings struggling to keep it aloft.

Articuno lost the exchange.

"Finish it, Ninetales—Iron Tail!" Johan commanded.

Ninetales dashed forward, its nine tails gleaming with metallic brilliance. With graceful yet powerful movements, it struck Articuno repeatedly.

Thud!

Articuno crashed to the ground, fainted.

Chat Exploded:

"She won! Ninetales actually beat Articuno!"

"Legendary Whisperer Johan strikes again!"

"Ninetales supremacy!"

Thorton, holding Articuno's Pokéball, chuckled. "You and your Ninetales make an incredible team. You've earned this."

He handed Johan the Knowledge Symbol, the emblem of the Battle Factory.

"Thanks," Johan said, smiling as he exchanged a victorious glance with Ninetales.

But Thorton offered a final warning. "If you're planning to conquer the Battle Frontier, you'll need to face Brandon. His Regi team is on a whole different level."

Johan's smile faltered slightly. Trust me, I know.

As they exited the factory, Johan spotted Gengar, Tinkaton, Kubfu, and Jirachi sitting on a flowerbed outside, each happily eating an ice cream cone.

"Where... did you get those?" Johan asked suspiciously.

Gengar scratched its head sheepishly and explained it had been a gift from a bald old man with a white beard and a cane.

"What... did you do?" Johan pressed.

Gengar shrugged nonchalantly, tossing an ice cream wrapper over its shoulder.

Meanwhile, Steven received a phone call. His expression instantly stiffened.

# 308: The Sea Cave and the Temple Ruins (BONUS)

Another quiet night passed uneventfully.

The next morning, Johan gathered his Pokémon in the yard, a rare seriousness in his demeanor.

"Teacher seems unusually focused today," Flannery remarked, resting her chin on her hand under the parasol.

Steven nodded. "He has good reason to be. Today, he's facing the Battle Frontier's strongest brain, Brandon, in the Battle Pyramid."

Roxanne added, "Brandon's strength is unmatched, even among the Frontier Brains. He's the one who's captured the three legendary titans."

In the yard, Johan stood in the middle of a semi-circle of his Pokémon, his gaze sharp as he addressed them with gravity.

"Remember this: lamb will never beat beef. So at the very least, tonight, we're feasting on steak!"

"Ka-kah..."

Gengar muttered under its breath. "But roast lamb tastes amazing too..."

Johan caught that. "Alright, let's vote. All in favor of Gengar's roast lamb idea, raise your hands!"

With a dramatic flourish, Gengar shot its hand up.

When it turned to look behind, it found that Ninetales, Tinkaton, Dragonair, Kubfu, Charcadet, Rotom, and Jirachi hadn't budged. Its ears drooped, and it adopted a pitiful expression, fiddling with its fingers dejectedly.

Feeling guilty, Ninetales and the others hesitated, then slowly raised their paws and tails.

"Caught you!" Johan growled inwardly.

From Gengar's downward glance, Johan could see through its act.

"Alright, fine. Steak and lamb for everyone!"

The Pokémon erupted into cheers, Gengar leading the charge.

"Has Teacher found a strategy to win?" Flannery asked, noticing Johan's energy.

After breakfast, the group prepared to head to the Battle Pyramid. But they were informed that Brandon and the Battle Pyramid were no longer at the Battle Frontier.

The one to break the news was none other than Anabel, a Frontier Brain who worked closely with the International Police.

"We uncovered some critical leads from the captured Team Aqua members, as well as traces left behind. It led us to an operation site," Anabel explained via phone. "Brandon has already gone ahead with the Battle Pyramid."

Shortly after, Anabel arrived at their villa to pick up Johan, Steven, Flannery, and Roxanne.

While only Johan was meant to assist with the operation, Steven and the others insisted on joining since they had been involved.

The group boarded a small aircraft that took them to a remote island.

When the plane landed, they saw the high-tech Battle Pyramid parked in an open clearing.

The pyramid, both a battle facility and a mobile command center, exuded an air of technological superiority.

Brandon was there, directing operations with precision.

The group approached him, noticing several subdued individuals being escorted onto a plane by the International Police.

"Looks like we arrived late," Anabel remarked.

"Anabel, Johan," Brandon greeted them with a nod, his typically stoic demeanor softening slightly.

"What's the situation?" Flannery asked, glancing at the detainees. "Are those Team Aqua members?"

Brandon shook his head. "No, but they might as well be. They're poachers. This island is one of their bases."

Poachers?

Johan frowned, observing the captives.

It seemed too coincidental to track Team Aqua's movements only to stumble upon a poacher hideout.

Were the two groups connected, or was Team Aqua using the poachers as a diversion?

Brandon explained that dismantling the poachers' operation was a priority, as it could lead to taking down their entire network.

Johan's thoughts turned to the island's location.

"Anabel, which route is this island on?"

"Route 128," she replied without hesitation.

Route 128?!

Johan's heart skipped a beat.

Route 128 was a highly sensitive area in Hoenn, as it was home to the Seafloor Cavern, the underwater resting place of Kyogre and Groudon.

The implications were clear: the poachers were likely connected to Team Aqua, who might be using them as pawns in a larger scheme.

"Something wrong, Johan?" Anabel asked, noticing his contemplative look.

Johan shook his head. He couldn't explain his concerns, but he was sure of one thing: Kyogre and Groudon wouldn't awaken without their respective orbs.

As Johan wandered the island, he stumbled upon a hidden ruin embedded within the landscape.

The ruin was partially overgrown with vines, its entrance concealed by dense foliage. Broken pillars and weathered statues hinted at its ancient origins.

"These markings... someone's tampered with this site," Johan muttered, examining the remains.

Brandon joined him, explaining that the poachers had been hiding in the ruins before their capture.

"Does Johan have experience with ruins?" Brandon asked, curious.

"A little," Johan replied modestly.

Gengar suddenly popped its head out of the ruin's staircase, holding a peculiar stone basin.

"Ka-kah! Look at this! It's got a rock inside!"

Brandon frowned, recognizing the artifact. "Where did you find that?"

Gengar pointed deeper into the ruins, explaining that it had found a secret chamber with a pedestal holding the basin.

"A secret chamber?" Brandon murmured, intrigued.

Johan chuckled. "Gengar has a knack for finding interesting rocks."

Gengar handed the basin to Brandon, but kept the small rock inside for itself. It spat on the rock and wiped it clean, revealing a faint reddish hue beneath the grime.

"A gem?" Johan asked, inspecting the stone.

Gengar shrugged. "Don't think so. It's just heavy."

Curious, Johan asked, "What did the chamber look like?"

Using Hypnosis, Gengar projected its memory into Johan's mind.

The chamber was dark and spacious, centered around a pedestal holding the basin. Behind the pedestal was a wall carved with intricate patterns.

The carvings depicted a massive creature—one that couldn't fly.

Johan's stomach sank.

This ruin was a temple dedicated to Groudon.

He glanced at the reddish stone in Gengar's hand.

Could this be... a fragment of Groudon itself?

The connection brought to mind the Magma Rock, a fragment of Groudon's body once used by Butler in an attempt to create a superweapon.

"Gengar, you might've uncovered something big," Johan muttered.

Brandon broke the silence. "Johan, since the Battle Pyramid is already here, why not have your final Frontier challenge here?"

Johan hesitated, but Brandon explained that he couldn't return to the Battle Frontier anytime soon due to the ongoing operation.

As Johan considered, Gengar smugly polished the stone in its hand, oblivious to the weight of its discovery.

# 309: Johan vs. Brandon! The Titan Appears! (BONUS)

The idea of battling atop ruins associated with Groudon and Kyogre was unusual, but Johan couldn't help finding the situation somewhat ironic.

However, Groudon and Kyogre slumbered far below the sea in their caverns, far beyond the reach of ordinary submarines. Even if someone tried to disturb them, they wouldn't wake so easily. If it were that simple, leaders like Archie and Maxie would have succeeded long ago.

Brandon seemed intent on thoroughly investigating the ruins. Since he wouldn't be returning to the Battle Frontier anytime soon, Johan agreed to battle here.

"Alright," Johan nodded.

This way, the battle wouldn't interfere with dinner plans.

When Steven, Flannery, and Roxanne learned the battle would be on-site, they were surprised.

"What about the live broadcast?" Flannery asked.

Steven shrugged. "Mr. Scott has been so busy lately. It might be for the best."

The surge of visitors to the Battle Frontier due to Johan's popularity, the comet's appearance, and the successful livestream had Scott swamped.

"Besides, the series has already been a huge success," Steven added.

Anabel, intrigued by the upcoming battle, decided to stay and oversee the match after sending the captured poachers back to base.

Brandon's Battle Pyramid opened its upper tier, revealing a large battleground.

"Johan, smash him! Johan, smash him!"

Jirachi, free from pretending to be an ordinary Pokémon, waved its tiny fists with enthusiasm.

Johan sighed, rubbing his temples.

"It's Gengar's fault Jirachi's picked up this behavior," he muttered inwardly.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar smirked mischievously, ruffling Jirachi's hair in approval.

The battle was set to be a 3-on-3 rotation match, with Anabel acting as the referee.

Brandon, ever serious, swiftly entered battle mode.

Johan mirrored his intensity.

Brandon was the first to act. "Go, Dusknoir!"

Out came a ghostly Pokémon wrapped in gray bandages, its single red eye gleaming ominously.

"Dusknoir, the Gripper Pokémon, Ghost-type. Its hollow body is said to trap wandering souls. Staring into its eye is said to lead to doom!"

Even RotomDex hesitated to look directly at Dusknoir, while Gengar turned its head away in mild fear.

Johan chose Ninetales as his opener. The elegant Ice Pokémon gracefully unfurled its tails, summoning a swirling snowfall.

"A solid start from Johan," Roxanne noted.

Flannery pouted. "I wanted to see a Ghost vs. Ghost match between Dusknoir and Gengar."

"Let's begin!" Anabel declared.

"Dusknoir, Fire Punch!"

Brandon crossed his arms, issuing his command with authority.

Dusknoir's massive fists ignited with flames as it charged toward Ninetales.

"Aurora Veil!"

Ninetales swiftly conjured layers of shimmering light within the falling snow.

Dusknoir's fiery fists clashed against the barriers, shattering layer after layer. Its brute strength was terrifying.

"That's incredible!" Flannery gasped.

Even Roxanne was impressed. "Breaking through multiple layers of Aurora Veil with pure force... Brandon's Dusknoir is something else."

Ninetales barely managed to maintain the final layer of protection. It exhaled softly, relieved.

"Ninetales, Dark Pulse!"

Ninetales unleashed a wave of dark energy, crashing directly into Dusknoir.

But the attack struck only an afterimage.

"Substitute!"

Dusknoir reappeared behind Ninetales, its eye glowing malevolently.

"Now, Confuse Ray!"

Brandon seized the opportunity.

Dusknoir began emitting a disorienting light.

"Seal it!" Johan countered.

[Imprison: Prevents opponents from using moves that the user knows.]

Ninetales emitted a soft psychic glow, immediately cutting off Dusknoir's Confuse Ray.

Brandon and Dusknoir froze in surprise.

Johan didn't let the opening slip.

"Blizzard!"

Ninetales unleashed a fierce snowstorm, engulfing Dusknoir.

"Dusknoir, surround yourself with Will-O-Wisp!"

Brandon attempted to mitigate the damage by creating a fiery shield.

But Ninetales had no intention of letting up.

The storm intensified, enhanced by its psychic energy. When the snow cleared, Dusknoir was frozen solid, flames suspended mid-air within the ice.

"What power..." Brandon murmured.

Roxanne shook her head. "The turning point was Imprison. It completely disrupted Dusknoir's strategy."

Brandon recalled Dusknoir and sent out his second Pokémon.

"Go, Solrock!"

The levitating Sun Pokémon emerged, and Brandon wasted no time.

"Iron Head!"

Johan quickly recalled Ninetales, sending out Dragonair in its place.

"Dragonair, Extreme Speed!"

The nimble Dragon Pokémon narrowly avoided Solrock's rapid assault.

Brandon's eyes gleamed with respect. "Quick reflexes..."

Solrock immediately followed up. "Stone Edge!"

Sharp rocks erupted from the ground.

"Fly up and Dragon Dance!" Johan countered.

Dragonair danced gracefully in the air, evading the rocky spikes and boosting its power and speed.

Brandon wasn't deterred. "Rock Slide!"

Large boulders rained from above, slamming Dragonair into the ground.

Johan winced. Solrock's attack speed was impressive.

Brandon pressed the advantage. "Earthquake!"

The ground trembled violently, but Dragonair responded quickly.

"Extreme Speed, then Dragon Rush!"

Dragonair charged forward with blinding speed, cloaked in a dragon-shaped aura.

"Block it with Rock Tomb!"

Solrock used its psychic powers to create a barrier of rocks, but Dragonair smashed through it.

The clash sent both Pokémon crashing to the ground.

As the dust settled, Dragonair emerged, battered but victorious.

"Solrock is unable to battle!" Anabel declared.

Flannery cheered. "Two down!"

Roxanne wasn't as optimistic. "Brandon still has his ace..."

Brandon smiled faintly, recalling Solrock.

"Johan, your bond with your Pokémon is extraordinary. Now, let's see how you handle this!"

He threw his final Poké Ball.

"Registeel, stand firm!"

With a mischievous laugh, Gengar melded into the shadows, evading the attack entirely.

"Registeel, behind you!" Brandon shouted.

Gengar emerged from Registeel's shadow, holding a faintly glowing orb of blue static—Thunder Wave!

But Registeel stomped the ground hard, raising a slab of rock to block the attack.

Brandon smirked. "Earthquake!"

"Will-O-Wisp!"

The ground rumbled violently as Gengar was knocked back, clutching its sides. But the eerie blue flames it released had taken effect, burning Registeel and halving its attack power.

"That burn was crucial..." Flannery murmured.

Roxanne nodded. "If not for that, Earthquake might've knocked Gengar out entirely."

"Is Johan planning to use Destiny Bond again?" Roxanne speculated.

The thought crossed Anabel's mind as well. She vividly remembered Gengar's Destiny Bond tactic against her Metagross during their battle at the Battle Tower.

"No need for that," Johan thought, meeting Gengar's gaze.

"Gengar, ready?"

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar's wide grin widened further. It slammed a foot down dramatically, dark energy swirling around it.

Brandon's eyes narrowed as he felt an ominous shift in the air.

"This energy... could it be...?"

A brilliant light formed between Johan and Gengar. From Johan's hand, the Key Stone on his glasses emitted a radiant glow, connecting with the stone embedded in Gengar's chest.

"Mega Evolution..."

A dazzling transformation ensued as Gengar's body underwent a spectacular change. The once-playful specter now stood as a terrifying force, its colors deepened, its presence heavier, and its aura overwhelming.

"Mega Gengar!"

The sheer intensity of Mega Gengar's Ghost energy made even Brandon take a step back. Registeel, though stoic, seemed momentarily shaken by the oppressive aura.

"Registeel, Lock-On!"

Brandon commanded, his voice steady despite the tension.

Golden crosshairs appeared in Registeel's vision, locking onto Mega Gengar's position.

"Thunderbolt!"

But before the move could fire, Johan and Gengar acted as one.

"Shadow Ball!"

A massive sphere of dark energy materialized above Mega Gengar, its size and intensity dwarfing any Shadow Ball seen before.

The world seemed to hold its breath as the two attacks collided.

BOOM!

The explosion rocked the Battle Pyramid, sending debris flying and shattering the reinforced glass. Dust filled the air as the two trainers steadied themselves.

When the dust settled, Registeel lay motionless, its armor scorched and cracked.

"Registeel is unable to battle! The winner is Johan!" Anabel declared.

Cheers erupted from the sidelines. Jirachi spun happily in the air.

Johan and Gengar exchanged tired but triumphant smiles.

"We did it."

Gengar gave a playful thumbs-up.

Brandon stepped forward, recalling Registeel. Despite his loss, a smile crept onto his face.

"Johan, your bond with your Pokémon is remarkable. Mega Evolution... that was extraordinary."

Johan nodded, still catching his breath. "Thanks. It's not perfect yet, but we're getting there."

As the audience applauded, Johan turned to his team, already thinking about their next challenge.

# 311: Conquering the Battle Frontier

"So strong..." Roxanne murmured.

The power of that Shadow Ball was overwhelming—it felt like her Pokémon wouldn't have survived even with ten lives.

Registeel had fallen.

"So... did Johan win?" Flannery muttered softly.

The battle felt surreal.

Registeel, God of Steel, was leagues stronger than Darach's Articuno from the day before.

But the true shock wasn't just Johan's victory—it was Gengar's Mega Evolution and the devastating Shadow Ball that followed.

Mega Evolution!

Was this power unique to Gengar?

Everyone present—Steven, Roxanne, Flannery, Anabel, and even Brandon—was left stunned by Gengar's transformation.

The Mega Evolution itself was surprising enough, but the Shadow Ball's sheer force was something they couldn't comprehend.

"So strong..." Anabel whispered, marveling at the display.

She suddenly realized: during their Battle Tower match, Johan had held back.

Otherwise, her Metagross wouldn't have stood a chance.

"Was I just a baby in Johan's eyes?"

The group quietly settled on a new metric:

If Johan didn't use Mega Evolution against you, you were officially a baby trainer.

Brandon approached Johan after recalling his fallen Registeel.

"You've won," he said solemnly.

"I don't fully understand what this 'Mega Evolution' is, but I can feel it—it's a testament to the deep bond between you and your Pokémon."

"You've shattered the final barrier."

He retrieved a golden emblem from his pocket.

"This 'Symbol of Courage' is yours."

"Ka-kah!"

With a wide grin, Gengar eagerly accepted the badge.

It knew these Frontier Symbols weren't particularly valuable, but this one was a hard-earned trophy. Johan had poured everything into this battle—even using Mega Evolution. The symbol was well-deserved.

Johan chuckled, shaking his head.

Mega Evolution wasn't something he could use casually. Even with his current endurance, the aftermath left him feeling drained.

"Gengar, pass me a piece."

"Ka-kah?"

Gengar tilted its head, rummaged in its bag, and handed Johan a chunk of a Sitrus Berry candy.

Johan popped it into his mouth and chewed.

"Hm... not bad. Sweet."

Now he understood why Gengar loved Sitrus candies but avoided energy roots—they weren't as tasty.

"Teacher!"

"Johan!"

The crowd of spectators—Steven, Roxanne, Flannery, Anabel, and Jirachi—all rushed toward Johan.

"Johan! You're amazing!"

Jirachi squealed in delight, launching itself into Johan's arms.

Johan laughed, giving the little Pokémon's cheek a playful pinch. "So soft... like a marshmallow."

Jirachi pouted but remained cuddly, nuzzling against Johan.

The warmth of their bond was palpable.

In truth, Johan had considered using Jirachi against Brandon if all three Titans were sent out. But forcing Jirachi into battle wasn't something he was willing to do.

This little one deserved peace after its millennium-long slumber.

Jirachi seemed to sense Johan's thoughts. Even as he pinched its cheek, it clung to him affectionately.

With the final Symbol of Courage in hand, Johan and his team had officially conquered the Battle Frontier.

On the Return Flight

Flannery couldn't contain her curiosity.

"Teacher, what is Mega Evolution?"

Johan glanced at her, gently stroking Jirachi's head. "Mega Evolution... well, it's something Professor Sycamore has been researching extensively. It's somewhat similar to Galar's Dynamax phenomenon."

He explained the basics of Mega Evolution—how it required specific Pokémon, a Mega Stone for the Pokémon, a Key Stone for the trainer, and most importantly, a deep bond between them.

Anabel's eyes widened. "So it's not just about items—it's about the bond too?"

Johan nodded. "Exactly. Without that bond, Mega Evolution might fail or even harm both the trainer and Pokémon."

He briefly mentioned Ash and Greninja's "Bond Phenomenon," which operated on a similar principle.

Steven leaned forward. "Which Pokémon can Mega Evolve?"

Johan chuckled. "It's still under research, but... your Metagross can."

Steven's eyes lit up with excitement. "Metagross... can Mega Evolve?"

Flannery gawked. "Steven's already terrifying with Metagross. If it Mega Evolves, it's game over!"

Johan laughed. "That's the nature of Mega Evolution—it raises the stakes. For example, Cynthia's Garchomp and Aggron can Mega Evolve too."

Roxanne looked thoughtful. "Doesn't this mean champion-level trainers will get even stronger?"

Johan nodded. "Precisely. Mega Evolution adds a new dimension to battles. It's a game-changer."

Flannery smirked. "Poor Lance. No Mega Evolution for him?"

The group burst into laughter, imagining Lance's dismay.

As the conversation continued, Johan dropped hints about other Pokémon capable of Mega Evolution—Blaziken, Lucario, Houndoom, and more.

Back at the Battle Frontier

Palmer, the Battle Frontier owner, was stunned when he arrived at Brandon's island.

The sight of the battered Battle Pyramid left him speechless.

"You used all three Titans, right?" Palmer asked Brandon.

Brandon nodded solemnly. "I did."

"And... you still lost?"

Brandon's expression remained serious. "Yes. Johan was simply stronger."

Palmer was incredulous. "Wait... was it close? Did you at least take down one of his Pokémon?"

Brandon shook his head. "No. It was 3–0. He swept me."

Palmer felt faint.

News of Johan's victory spread like wildfire. Headlines celebrated his conquest of the Battle Frontier.

"Johan, the Rising Star of a New Generation!"

"Three Days to Dominate the Battle Frontier!"

Meanwhile, far away in Unova, a young trainer named Tobias stepped out of his house, only to be mobbed by reporters.

"What do you think of Johan's victory?"

Tobias adjusted his cap, his expression calm. "I'm a fan of his."

# 312: An Invitation to Become a Battle Frontier Brain

Deep within the ocean, aboard a submarine,

Dock idly scrolled through his phone as he followed orders to lay low for a while.

Upon seeing the news of Johan defeating Brandon with a 3–0 victory, even besting the Legendary Titans, Dock wiped the sweat from his brow.

"Good thing I ran fast enough…" he muttered with relief.

Viridian City, Rocket Base

"Matori, bring up the latest news on Johan," Giovanni ordered.

The large screen displayed Matori's image alongside recent news of Johan conquering the Battle Frontier and defeating Brandon.

"Already capable of defeating Brandon?" Giovanni murmured, visibly impressed.

"His progress is remarkable."

When Johan first visited the Viridian Gym, his unique insights and talents were evident, but his actual battle strength had been fairly average.

Yet, in just a few short months, Johan had grown strong enough to conquer the Battle Frontier and face Brandon head-on.

Giovanni, however, was no stranger to Brandon's strength.

"By the way," he added, "we haven't given Johan his year-end bonus yet, have we?"

"Not yet," Matori confirmed.

"Make the arrangements," Giovanni instructed.

"Understood!"

Hoenn Region, Rustboro Pokémon Academy

In the principal's office, the elderly headmaster chuckled as he spoke on the phone.

"Clavell, there's no need to worry—Johan will surely bring a few students to visit your academy for an exchange program."

Behind him, a Pangoro chewed on a bamboo stick, contemplating how it could best spar with the old man today.

"And that favor I asked for?"

"Ah, you mean Professor Raifort?"

The headmaster's mind wandered to a certain figure.

Raifort, a historian at Naranja Academy, had previously been an adventurer involved in archaeology and tomb raiding.

"Ancient blueprints, you say? That's tricky... but doable. Better than nothing, I suppose."

After hanging up, the headmaster stroked his chin.

"Ancient armor schematics, huh? Could Johan really forge them himself?"

"Roar!"

The Pangoro cracked its knuckles, flashing a grin. It was time to train!

The old headmaster stood, his body trembling slightly, but his face filled with confidence.

"Come on, let me teach you a new move today!"

From the principal's office, the sound of punches and loud grunts echoed.

Johan stretched lazily in bed, scrolling through his phone.

"Woke up to find the internet turning me into a Pokémon Master…" he muttered, amused by the headlines.

Various media outlets had taken his recent achievements and blown them out of proportion.

Some articles claimed:

"Brandon rivals the Elite Four. Johan crushed him 3–0. Ergo, Johan could crush the Elite Four!"

Even more ridiculous were jokes like:

"Since Brandon can't beat Johan, and I can't beat Johan, does that mean I'm equal to Brandon?"

Such exaggerated claims made Johan chuckle.

As for comparisons to the Elite Four, Johan shrugged it off.

Yes, he had defeated Brandon's Registeel, but only with Ninetales' Baton Pass buffs and Gengar's Mega Evolution.

By the end of that battle, both he and Gengar were utterly spent, barely able to stand.

He knew well that there was still a significant gap between him and the Elite Four.

"Guess some people just don't want to see me compete in the League…"

Johan wasn't bothered by public opinion.

As a registered Junior Trainer, he was already punching above his weight by competing in the League.

Besides, he thought with a grin, who cares about public opinion when your goal is fun, challenge, and adventure?

After a long morning of lounging in bed, Johan finally got up, washed, and stepped outside into the garden.

The Pokémon were already playing.

"Gengar! Mega Evolution!"

Excited, Jirachi cheered.

"Ka-kah!" Gengar growled in mock seriousness, mist swirling around it as it mimicked its Mega form.

"Tinkaton handed over a paper eye, and Gengar stuck it to its forehead, pretending it was part of its transformation.

The Pokémon burst into laughter.

"Draaa?"

Dragonair tilted its head. "Gengar, where do your feet go during Mega Evolution?"

Gengar froze. "???"

It had never considered that before.

Johan, noticing the scene, chuckled and stepped in.

"Mega Gengar's body partially shifts into a different dimension. Its lower half hides there, ready to dodge at any moment."

The Pokémon nodded, enlightened.

Relieved, Gengar wiped imaginary sweat from its brow.

"Gengar, show me the stone you found yesterday," Johan asked.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar retrieved the gray-black stone and handed it over. Johan noticed its surface had been wiped clean, revealing faint streaks of crimson.

"Goka?"

Charcadet was immediately drawn to the stone, sensing a strange energy emanating from it.

Johan raised an eyebrow. Could this stone be connected to Groudon?

"Whatever it is, it's just an inanimate object now."

Smiling, he handed the stone to Charcadet.

"If it resonates with you, then it's yours."

Charcadet's flames flickered, glowing a slightly deeper red as it carefully stored the stone away.

"Johan, Mr. Scott wants to see you," Flannery and Roxanne said, approaching the garden.

Scott arrived, dressed in his usual Alola-style beachwear.

After congratulating Johan on conquering the Battle Frontier, Scott extended an invitation.

"As per tradition, any trainer who collects all seven Gold Symbols and conquers the Battle Frontier is eligible to become a Frontier Brain. Would you be interested, Johan?"

Johan hesitated.

Scott's enthusiasm grew.

"If you agree, we can even design a new facility for you—the Symbol of Mindfulness, perhaps?"

The idea intrigued Gengar, who was practically glowing at the thought of such prestige.

But Johan shook his head, smiling.

"Thanks, Scott, but I think I'll pass."

Becoming a Frontier Brain would mean giving up his freedom to teach, travel, and bond with Pokémon. For now, Johan preferred his current path.

"If you ever change your mind, the Battle Frontier will always welcome you," Scott said.

After Scott departed, Johan turned to the Pokémon.

"Alright, everyone! No training today—we're going to the amusement park!"

Cheers erupted as the Pokémon celebrated the promise of a fun-filled day.

# 313: The Disappearing Comet and Johan’s Wish

"Jirachi, don't eat too much ice cream, or you'll get a stomachache," Johan warned.

[Alright…]

Jirachi reluctantly looked at the vanilla ice cream in its left hand and the creamy milk ice cream in its right. It couldn't decide which to give up.

Seeing its hesitant and conflicted expression, Johan sighed.

"Fine, just eat them both."

As a mythical Pokémon, Jirachi probably wouldn't actually get a stomachache from eating too much ice cream.

[Yay!]

The clever little Pokémon twinkled its eyes mischievously and happily devoured the treats.

Johan shook his head, smiling.

Thanks to the increase in visitors to the Battle Frontier and the attraction of the Millennium Comet, the streets were vibrant and festive. Shops had sprung up, and the roads were decorated with bright lights, giving the area the feel of a grand celebration.

The group strolled along the bustling streets, stopping occasionally to play games or buy snacks.

"Ka-kah~~"

Suddenly, Gengar tugged at Jirachi's ribbon and put a finger to its lips, signaling it to stay quiet.

Jirachi's eyes sparkled, and it quickly covered its mouth with its hands, nodding excitedly.

The two snuck away for a moment.

When they returned, they were draped in a large white sheet. Moving silently, they crept up behind Johan.

"Ka-kah!!"

Gengar yelled as it ripped off the sheet, revealing itself grinning mischievously. Next to it stood Jirachi, dressed in a Gengar-themed costume, arms spread wide in imitation.

Johan: "…"

"Gengar, you'll even 'borrow' Jirachi's 'skin,' huh?"

Though Johan had noticed them earlier, when he saw Jirachi's expectant gaze, he paused for a moment before pretending to be startled.

"Ah! You scared me!" Johan said, clutching his chest dramatically.

[Hahaha!]

Jirachi laughed happily, rolling around on Gengar's head.

[We scared Johan! We scared him!]

"Ka-kah-kah!"

Gengar crossed its arms, looking triumphant. Its expression screamed, See how great I am? I'm the king of fear!

After more games and wandering, they finally returned to their lodging. Johan felt utterly exhausted.

Walking around with excitable Pokémon was far more tiring than a date.

Even as a capable fighter, he couldn't keep up with their boundless energy.

When they arrived, Johan discovered Steven, Flannery, and Roxanne sitting in the garden, playing cards.

"Seriously?" Johan muttered.

Seeing the game, Johan's interest was piqued.

"Move aside, Flannery. Let me show you how it's done," Johan said, rolling up his sleeves.

"Fine, go ahead," Flannery replied with a smirk, giving up her seat.

Johan quickly learned the card game's rules.

"One pair of Caterpie!" Johan declared dramatically, throwing down his cards.

"Bomb!" Steven replied, slamming down two Rayquaza cards.

"…But I only played Caterpie…" Johan blinked.

Steven grinned. "I know," he said, laying down a seven-card straight.

"…Pass," Roxanne said, refraining from challenging her teammate's move.

Johan glanced at his hand, then at Steven's.

"Alright," Johan sighed, gathering the cards. "Let's see you handle all 17 of mine!"

"Straight," Steven declared again, playing eight cards in a row.

Johan: "…"

The garden erupted with laughter.

Even Jirachi, who didn't understand the game, joined in the mirth.

Determined to win, Johan called upon his Pokémon for help.

"Ninetales, help me draw cards!"

The next day marked Jirachi's seventh day of awakening.

As the group left the Battle Frontier, they returned to the stone pillar forest where they had camped before.

The night sky sparkled with countless stars, and the Millennium Comet stretched across the heavens.

"Sleeping for a thousand years, only to wake for seven days…" Flannery murmured, her voice tinged with sadness as she looked at Jirachi.

"It's so unfair," Roxanne added, her expression somber.

[Huh?]

Jirachi, holding a wish stick it had bought from a shop, tilted its head in confusion.

The shopkeeper had claimed that waving the stick at the comet would make any wish come true. Ironically, the one who bought it was the Wish Pokémon itself—and it genuinely believed the claim.

Steven smiled at Jirachi. "It doesn't matter how long it sleeps. What's important is that it's happy when it wakes."

[Everyone! Let's play together!]

Jirachi's soft glow melded with the starlit sky. Its ribbons fluttered as it smiled innocently.

Johan tucked away his phone, no longer checking the time.

"Alright," he said warmly. "Let's play."

As the night wore on, the group made their way to their campsite.

Suddenly, the stars grew brighter.

Jirachi began to shine, floating upward. Its True Eye opened on its stomach, responding to the comet's light.

The Millennium Comet's glow enveloped Jirachi, bathing the area in radiant energy.

"It's time to say goodbye," Johan said softly.

The Pokémon lowered their heads, visibly saddened. Even Gengar pulled out a handkerchief, pretending to wipe its eyes.

Flannery and Roxanne were teary-eyed, and even Steven looked melancholic.

[Johan…]

Jirachi's voice echoed in Johan's mind.

[You haven't made a wish yet. Whatever you wish for, Jirachi will grant it!]

Johan hesitated, then smiled gently.

"I wish for Jirachi to have beautiful dreams during its thousand years of sleep. Long, happy dreams that will keep you smiling."

Jirachi blinked, then smiled.

[Johan, I'm sleepy. Sing to me?]

Johan softly hummed a lullaby. Though not a great singer, his voice was familiar and comforting to Jirachi.

Gradually, Jirachi's movements slowed, and its glow dimmed.

As crystalline layers formed around it, Jirachi returned to its dormant state.

"Ka-kah~"

"Draaa~"

Gengar, Ninetales, and the others waved as Jirachi disappeared beneath the earth, its glow fading into the ground.

At the same time, the comet vanished, leaving behind a sky full of brilliant stars.

Suddenly, a small fragment fell from the sky, glowing faintly green.

The fragment landed softly in Johan's hands.

It was Jirachi's final gift.

[Johan… remember me, okay? I'll feel it.]
