# 333: The Three Sinnoh Elite!

Celestic Town. Cynthia's Home.

"You can stay in the same room as before; nothing's really been moved."

Cynthia sat curled up on the couch, her arms hugging her knees, her snow-white feet peeking out and lightly swaying like small fish in clear water.

"Got it."

It was convenient, after all. Johan was already familiar with the place since this was his second visit.

After putting his luggage in the room, he stepped out and found Cynthia looking at him with a pitiful expression, her large gray eyes almost irresistible.

But thinking of Garchomp's sharp sideways glare and Lucario's watchful aura…

Johan gritted his teeth and coldly declared, "I'll charge extra for that!"

"Okay~~" Cynthia obediently replied, her voice sweet.

"Hmph!"

Scoffing, Johan grabbed an apron from the wall, tied it around himself, and asked, "What do you want to eat?"

Cynthia tilted her head, blinking as though deep in thought, her hesitation and indecision written all over her face.

Johan sighed. He really shouldn't have asked.

Getting someone with choice paralysis to pick a meal? By the time she made up her mind, they'd probably need to think about tomorrow's breakfast instead.

"Noodles?"

Quick, simple, and practical.

Besides, given how rarely Cynthia stayed at home, there probably weren't many ingredients in the kitchen anyway.

Just then, Johan's phone buzzed twice.

Beep! Beep!

A notification popped up: 10,000 Pokédollars received.

He clicked his tongue in satisfaction.

"Let's do noodles, then," Cynthia said with a smile.

Johan's mood instantly brightened. Grinning, he said, "Right away, dear guest!"

Humming a tune, he grabbed Rotom and headed into the kitchen.

"Lo…to?"

Rotom tilted its screen-like face, perplexed.

With pay secured, Johan's motivation soared.

Meanwhile in the Living Room…

Lucario glanced at Charcadet, who had quietly walked up to it.

"Lu...ca?"

Its tone carried curiosity.

With a faint hum, Charcadet released a small wave of aura, signaling its intent.

Lucario immediately understood—it had come seeking guidance.

For a student with a humble and studious attitude, Lucario was always happy to oblige, just as it had once taught Tinkaton.

Elsewhere, Gengar had dragged Garchomp off to the side.

"Ka-kah~~"

It glanced sneakily toward the kitchen, ensuring Johan and Rotom were preoccupied before whispering conspiratorially:

"Big Sis Shark, what's the plan for tonight?"

Hearing this, the other Pokémon in the room perked up their ears.

It had been a while since they'd had any nighttime "excursions."

Back in Rustboro City, they'd practically exhausted all the local wild and stray Pokémon.

Who didn't know the name of "Sunglasses Gengar" by now?

Now that they were at the resource-rich Mt. Coronet, how could they pass up the opportunity to stir up some chaos?

"Garrr!"

Garchomp huffed coldly, shooting Gengar a sidelong glare before curling its lip in a menacing grin.

"You'll find out by midnight."

Later

Johan emerged from the kitchen carrying two steaming bowls of udon noodles.

The savory aroma wafted through the air, making Cynthia's nose twitch. She perked up and walked to the dining table, her tired demeanor replaced with eager anticipation.

On a chilly winter night, especially one near Mt. Coronet, a simple bowl of assorted udon was perfect for banishing fatigue and warming the soul.

Setting the bowls down, Johan barely had time to adjust his apron before Cynthia clasped her hands together and declared:

"Time to eat!"

Johan shook his head with a wry smile.

Noticing Gengar's hopeful gaze, he said, "The fridge is almost empty. These two bowls were the limit. If you want some, I'll give you my portion."

"Ka-kah?" Gengar scratched its head in confusion.

"What about you?"

"Me? I'll just snack on some Pokéblocks."

Garchomp & Lucario: ??

Johan casually retrieved a box of Pokéblocks and distributed them to all the Pokémon present, including Cynthia's team.

Soon, the room was filled with the sound of contented munching as everyone dug into their meals.

In the end, Gengar didn't touch Johan's noodles—it was too worried about him stealing its Pokéblocks in retaliation.

Cynthia ate slowly, taking small, delicate bites. Yet somehow, her bowl emptied at a surprising pace.

When she slurped up the last of the broth, her cheeks flushed slightly, and she let out a soft, satisfied burp.

Realizing Johan was watching, she quickly averted her gaze, her blush deepening.

"To change the subject," she stammered, "I heard Rotom Pokémon are becoming more common recently?"

Johan blinked. "Probably because of the discovery of Rotom Island."

He recalled how, back when he first played the Pokémon games, he'd assumed Rotom was some kind of mythical Pokémon—especially since Cyrus had schemed to capture one.

Now?

Johan glanced at his own Rotom, currently alternating between biting a battery and nibbling a Pokéblock.

"Roto?"

Sensing his gaze, Rotom paused mid-bite, sparks flickering from its body.

"Nothing," Johan said.

"Roto!"

Rotom nodded blankly before resuming its feast.

"Apparently, they've even started making Rotom-powered appliances, like berry blenders," Cynthia added.

"Rotom berry blenders?" Johan raised an eyebrow.

"They're supposed to improve the quality of berries."

"That sounds useful. How much is one?"

"Hmm…" Cynthia tilted her head. "I'm not sure. A few million Pokédollars, maybe?"

Johan froze.

On second thought, his old, affordable berry blender was perfectly fine. It only cost around 100,000 Pokédollars and worked just fine for making Pokéblocks his Pokémon loved.

Later That Night

After finishing their meal, Johan and Cynthia stayed up chatting for a bit before finally heading to bed, yawning as they went.

For the Pokémon, however…

The night was just beginning.

The Next Morning

"Kuuu~!"

Kubfu stood before Mt. Coronet, bowing repeatedly with utmost sincerity.

"Thank you, great nature, for your generous bounty! This humble bear offers its deepest gratitude!"

Johan: …

He didn't even need to guess.

The wild Pokémon around Mt. Coronet were probably in for a harsh winter this year.

Pouring himself a cup of coffee, Johan opened the window, letting in the crisp morning air.

The cool breeze was invigorating, bringing with it the subtle scent of snow.

On the refrigerator, he found a note from Cynthia.

She had left early for the Celestic Town Historical Research Center, leaving Johan free to explore or train.

She'd even mentioned a training area south of the house, equipped with plenty of facilities.

"Perfect," Johan thought, smiling. "After breakfast, we'll train for a bit."

Stepping outside, he relished the soft feel of grass underfoot and the warm touch of sunlight.

Just as he was about to comment on the beauty of the morning, he froze.

His gaze shifted to a nearby tree, where three people stood, staring at him in stunned silence.

"That guy… just came out of the boss lady's house…" Flint stammered, his wild, fiery hair practically standing on end.

Aaron's antenna-like strand of hair quivered as he seemed to have an epiphany.

"It's Johan!"

Lucian, meanwhile, turned away, shielding half his face with his hand as he muttered, "I saw nothing. I saw nothing. I saw nothing…"

Johan blinked.

Wait a second.

These three were none other than three future members of Sinnoh's Elite Four: Flint, the Fire-type master; Aaron, the Bug-type specialist; and Lucian, the Psychic-type ace.

"What are you guys doing here?" Johan asked.

Aaron, eyes sparkling, exclaimed, "You're Johan!"

Lucian groaned. "Please don't lump me in with these two…"

Flint's jaw dropped, his disbelief growing. "You even know what the boss lady did this morning?!"

Aaron, however, grew even more excited. "It's really Johan!"

Johan: …

He glanced at Lucian, offering a silent look of sympathy.

It must be exhausting hanging around these two all the time.

Lucian's eyes met his, glistening with unshed tears.

Finally, someone who understands…

"Johan!" Flint suddenly shouted, startling everyone.

Even Gengar, who had been napping inside, fell from the ceiling with a loud thud.

Rubbing its head, it floated out of the wall and glared at Flint.

"Ka-kah!! (ಠ益ಠ)"

What's with the yelling?!

But Flint ignored it, pointing at Johan with burning determination.

"Let's battle!"

Johan's face twitched.

"…No."

Flint froze, clearly unprepared for such a swift rejection.

Aaron gawked. "But battling is the best form of training!"

Johan exchanged a look with Gengar.

"Ka-kah~~"

"…He might have a point."

# 334: The “Parental Love” of Magmortar, Blazing Aura Burst

Celestic Town – Cynthia's Training Field

Johan had to admit, Flint wasn't entirely wrong.

After all, nothing accelerated growth like battles.

In the games, battling a rival like Flint usually meant your Pokémon would level up at least three times after one round.

In reality, battling tested tactics and moves in ways that traditional training simply couldn't replicate.

"Johan, be careful. Flint may be brash, but he's no pushover," Lucian cautioned, pushing up his glasses.

Johan nodded. Of course, he knew this.

In the Pokémon Adventures manga, Flint and Volkner's teamwork was strong enough to go toe-to-toe with Giratina, even if only briefly.

But...

"This is just a practice match. There's no need to go all out," Johan said with a faint smile.

Hearing this, Flint scratched his head in confusion.

"Is there really such a thing as not taking a battle seriously?"

For him, battling a strong opponent meant going all in, every time.

"I'll referee," Lucian volunteered, adjusting his brown-tinted glasses as they glinted slightly in the light.

He'd long wanted to see Johan's so-called "master tactician" skills in action. Watching his battles on TV wasn't the same as seeing them live. Now was the perfect opportunity to learn.

Meanwhile, Aaron finally caught on to what was happening. He clapped his hands together and exclaimed, "Wait, Flint's battling Johan?!"

This reaction speed... It made Johan wonder if Aaron's eventual Elite Four position would only come from Sinnoh running out of candidates.

Lucian took charge, announcing, "Both sides, call out your Pokémon."

With a hearty laugh, Flint grabbed a Pokéball.

"Johan, I've been waiting for this battle for a long time!"

He had watched Johan's record-breaking run through the Battle Frontier, almost in its entirety.

Although Flint missed Johan's final match against Brandon, he was inspired enough to try the Sinnoh Battle Frontier himself.

With his Infernape leading the charge, Flint nearly matched Johan's pace at the Battle Tower, earning an impressive streak.

But...

When he reached the Battle Factory, reality hit hard.

Flint's aggressive style worked wonders with his own Pokémon but fell apart when using unfamiliar rental teams.

The harsh lessons at the Battle Factory only made Flint want to battle Johan even more.

"This is our first battle, so I'm giving it everything I've got!" Flint declared, his fiery spirit flaring.

"Flint, how many times have you battled Cynthia?" Johan asked suddenly.

"Huh?"

Caught off guard, Flint scratched his head sheepishly. "Uh, about... 108 times?"

108 times...

Johan's eyebrow twitched.

"And how many of those did you win?"

"None!" Flint laughed loudly, though there was an awkward undertone.

His track record could be described as either "relentlessly persistent" or "persistently hopeless."

"Alright then," Johan said with a faint smile. "Let's battle."

"Oh!"

Flint was confused.

Lucian, however, tensed slightly.

What just happened?

With just two casual questions, Johan had extinguished the blazing fire that was Flint's trademark energy.

Lucian adjusted his glasses, studying Johan with admiration.

Is this the power of psychological tactics? He even dampened Flint's fighting spirit!

Flint, oblivious to the mental chess being played, released his first Pokémon: Magmortar.

The rotund, cannon-armed Fire-type emerged with a confident smirk, flexing its arm cannons as if to say, Bring it on.

"Magmortar, the Blast Pokémon. A pure Fire-type, its arm cannons can launch flames reaching up to 2000°C. Rotom reporting!"

Johan tapped his monocle lightly before selecting his own Pokéball.

He'd already considered Flint's potential lineup.

As a Fire-type specialist, Flint's Pokémon posed a significant challenge.

Johan's team, on the other hand, lacked the Water, Rock, or Ground-types needed to counter Fire-types effectively.

Still, this was just a practice match, and he wanted to gauge Flint's strength.

"Let's go, Charcadet!"

"Goka!!"

The little Fire-type appeared, its bird-like fiery crest flaring as it took an eager fighting stance.

Flint's eyes lit up.

"Your Charcadet, huh? I remember it from your Battle Frontier matches. Let's see how it handles the heat!"

Lucian adjusted his glasses again.

"Fire versus Fire. Interesting choice."

From the sidelines, Aaron was in awe.

"Whoa! Flint's Magmortar against Johan's Charcadet—this is gonna be epic!"

Lucian silently sighed, thinking, It's too early to call anything epic, Aaron...

The battle began.

"Magmortar, show them our firepower! Use Flamethrower!"

Magmortar's arm cannon glowed ominously before releasing a searing jet of flames.

"Charcadet, Flamethrower too," Johan ordered calmly.

"Goka!"

Charcadet stomped the ground, gathering fiery energy into its hands before unleashing a roaring stream of flames.

The two Flamethrowers collided, creating a swirling inferno that lit up the training field.

Even Lucian had to release his Mr. Mime to shield himself with a Light Screen.

The intense heat distorted the air, and Lucian murmured, "Is Johan also an emotion-driven battler? This level of firepower is... unexpected."

The flames clashed violently before Charcadet charged straight through the inferno.

Will-o'-the-Wisp Pathway.

Johan's tactical brilliance shone as Charcadet used the flame collision to mask its approach.

"Come on! Fire Punch!" Flint roared.

Magmortar's arm lit up, its fiery fist swinging down to meet the incoming Charcadet.

BAM!

Fist collided with fist, sending both Pokémon skidding back.

Flint grinned.

"Magmortar, Double Flamethrower!"

Magmortar raised both arm cannons, unleashing twin streams of fire.

Lucian raised an eyebrow. "That's unfairly efficient... it's like battling a two-in-one Pokémon."

Charcadet endured the flames, its fiery crest flaring brighter as it closed the distance again.

"Keep pressing, Charcadet! Will-o'-the-Wisp!"

"Goka!!"

Charcadet summoned ghostly green flames that danced toward Magmortar, forcing it to break its rhythm.

"Now, Incinerate!"

Charcadet's flames surged forward in a fiery cascade, catching Magmortar off guard.

Flint clenched his fists.

"Magmortar, use Stomp!"

Magmortar stomped the ground, causing a fiery shockwave to ripple outward.

Johan smirked.

"Charcadet, stand your ground!"

Charcadet weathered the impact, and a golden flame began to swirl around its body.

"What kind of fire is that?!" Aaron exclaimed.

Lucian's expression darkened.

"That's... the Flame of Life," he muttered.

"The what?"

"A sacred fire said to be connected to Ho-Oh and Entei, with restorative properties. Charcadet must have been blessed by Entei or Ho-Oh to wield it."

Johan's grin widened.

"Charcadet, finish this—Infernal Wave!"

Charcadet's entire body erupted in golden flames, the aura condensing into a spiraling wave of heat and light.

The radiant blaze engulfed Magmortar entirely, leaving Flint speechless.

When the flames subsided, Magmortar stood swaying before collapsing in defeat.

Flint's wide-eyed shock quickly gave way to fiery excitement.

"That... was incredible!"

Lucian adjusted his glasses.

"Johan has more than just tactics—he's turning Fire-types into an art form."

Flint laughed loudly.

"This battle isn't over yet! Let's go, Infernape!"

Johan smirked, summoning his next Pokémon.

"Well then... time for Round Two."

# 335: Johan VS Flint! Going All Out? Who Can't Do That?

Flint was fired up. After recalling his Magmortar, he wasted no time throwing out his second Poké Ball.

"Time for our next round. Go, Typhlosion!"

Emerging on the battlefield was a large, bipedal Pokémon resembling a badger, its neck ringed with roaring flames that surged and flickered violently as it roared.

"Typhlosion, the Volcano Pokémon, a pure Fire-type. When Typhlosion becomes furious, it burns everything it touches to ashes. Loto!"

Typhlosion?

When Johan saw Flint summon Typhlosion as his second Pokémon, he was a little surprised.

He had expected Flint to send out his signature Pokémon, Infernape, considering how fired up he was.

But after a moment's thought, it made sense.

In the early history of Sinnoh—particularly during the Hisui period—Pokémon like Typhlosion were present in the region.

Not to mention, Sinnoh and Johto shared a deep connection. Hundreds of years ago, some of Sinnoh's inhabitants migrated to ancient Johto, with the northernmost area of Johto still bearing the "Sinjoh Ruins" as proof of this shared history.

The bond between the two regions ran deep.

"Let's show Johan just how fierce our flames are!" Flint said, his excitement evident.

"Typhloooosion!!"

Typhlosion roared once more, the flames on its back erupting skyward, filling the area with scorching heat. The Pokémon's fighting spirit was blazing, as was Flint's.

Meanwhile, Johan withdrew Charcadet.

Although Charcadet had survived the powerful final Stomping Tantrum from Magmortar, it had taken too much damage to continue fighting.

Johan exhaled slowly, a calm smile forming on his face as his gaze sharpened.

Even Johan had to admit that battling an emotional trainer like Flint made it easy to be swept up in their energy.

When your opponent radiated such fiery intensity, it inevitably influenced you and your Pokémon as well.

Well then—nothing to hold back now.

Johan reached for the next Poké Ball.

"Why does it feel like Johan is getting fired up too?" muttered Aaron.

Lucian glanced at him, pushing his glasses up the bridge of his nose. "How did you feel when battling Flint?"

Aaron froze for a moment before realization dawned.

Of course.

When battling Flint, it was easy to find yourself drawn into his pace and emotions.

Pokémon battles weren't just exchanges between Pokémon; they were a form of communication between trainers as well.

Still, for a tactical trainer like Johan, falling into the rhythm of an emotional opponent could be a major disadvantage.

How would Johan handle this?

"Go, Dragonite!" Johan said.

From within a Luxury Ball, a streak of red light materialized into a Dragonite. Its entrance brought a fierce gust of wind that swept across the field.

Flint stood with his hands in his pockets, the breeze ruffling his spiky afro, but the fiery passion in his eyes remained undimmed.

His gaze locked onto the Dragonite, and he could feel the sheer power radiating from the pseudo-legendary Pokémon.

"So, it's Dragonite from the Kanto region."

Flint grinned.

"Now we're really getting somewhere!"

"Draaa!"

Dragonite looked at Typhlosion and Flint with a cheerful grin. It playfully thumped its fists together, the sound reverberating like a deep drum: Duang! Duang!

It, too, was ready for battle.

"Typhlosion, Flamethrower!" Flint roared.

"Typhloooosion!"

Typhlosion took a deep breath before expelling a stream of searing flames, surging forward like molten lava toward Dragonite.

Opening with Flamethrower—that was classic Typhlosion.

(Flamethrower: The move's power varies based on the user's HP. At full health, it's a scorching 150 power—the most potent non-exclusive Fire-type move.)

The blazing flames roared toward Dragonite, the sheer heat warping the air as they advanced.

"Dragonite, Dragon Dance to evade!" Johan ordered.

"Draaa!"

With two small but powerful flaps of its wings, Dragonite took to the air with surprising speed.

Its nimbleness was astonishing for its plump frame.

"The highest level of light-footedness is making even a large Pokémon move like the wind," Aaron commented, eyes wide.

"Follow it!" Flint shouted.

The Flamethrower twisted and turned, tracking the zigzagging Dragonite, painting glowing arcs in the sky.

"Use Hydro Pump!" Johan countered.

With a quick aerial turn, Dragonite fired a high-pressure jet of water from its mouth.

Hissssss—

The scorching flames collided with the water, producing a massive cloud of steam that quickly enveloped the battlefield.

"Stay sharp, Typhlosion!" Flint warned.

Swoosh—

A sound like slicing wind.

From within the steam, Dragonite emerged at breakneck speed, cloaked in blue-purple energy, diving directly at Typhlosion.

"Dragon Rush!"

"It's like Cynthia's Garchomp," Aaron murmured, stunned.

Flint and Typhlosion, however, didn't back down for a second.

Dodging? That wasn't their style.

"Typhlosion, one more Flamethrower!"

The molten firestorm erupted from Typhlosion's maw, surging forward once again.

BOOM!

The fire and Dragonite's charge collided, but—astonishingly—Dragonite ripped through the flames.

The fiery onslaught split apart like an unraveling thread as Dragonite barreled through it.

CRASH!

Dragonite's full-body tackle struck Typhlosion, shaking the ground and sending a plume of dust and debris into the air.

"D-did it just tear through Flamethrower?" Aaron stammered, his antenna-like strand of hair curling into a question mark.

Lucian, though startled, quickly pieced it together.

"Johan's Dragonite has the Multiscale ability," he explained.

(Multiscale: When the Pokémon is at full HP, the first attack it takes deals half damage.)

This meant Typhlosion's Flamethrower, though devastating, was effectively reduced to half its usual strength—further mitigated by Dragonite's natural resistance to Fire-type moves.

But even so, the sheer ferocity of the clash left both Lucian and Aaron astonished.

"Typhloooosion!!"

As the dust settled, Typhlosion stood in the middle of the impact zone, roaring ferociously as the flames on its back surged to their peak.

It had triggered its Blaze ability.

(Blaze: When the user's HP drops below a certain threshold, Fire-type moves gain a 1.5x damage multiplier.)

"Ohhhhh!!!" Flint roared. "Typhlosion, Overheat!"

Johan's eye twitched.

He could admit he was curious about the raw destructive power Flint's Typhlosion could unleash with Overheat in this state.

(Overheat: A Fire-type move with massive base power but at the cost of drastically reducing the user's Special Attack.)

But he wasn't about to find out—not like this.

"Extreme Speed!" Johan commanded.

"Draaa!"

Dragonite was surrounded by a white aura and shot forward like a streak of light, delivering a decisive strike.

WHAM!

Typhlosion collapsed with a heavy thud, its flames flickering out as it fainted.

Flint: "..."

Lucian: "..."

Aaron: "..."

For a moment, Flint looked like he'd swallowed a lump of charcoal. His lips moved, but no words came out.

Eventually, it was Lucian who broke the silence with an awkward chuckle.

"Well... that was... precise."

Honestly, it wasn't just precision—it was almost unfair. Extreme Speed had a higher priority than Overheat, allowing Dragonite to interrupt the attack entirely.

For Flint, it was the battle equivalent of gearing up to launch a massive fireball, only to get bopped in the face before the words "Fireball Jutsu" could leave his mouth.

It was worse than never getting fired up at all.

"Draaa!"

Dragonite flew back to Johan's side, looking sheepish as it rubbed the back of its head.

Was that too much?

Johan patted Dragonite on the arm, his smile sly.

"Don't worry. He's got something even stronger."

"Draa?"

Dragonite brightened, cracking its knuckles as its eyes gleamed with anticipation.

"Whew—"

Flint exhaled deeply as he recalled Typhlosion.

When he looked up again, his eyes were brighter than ever, his fiery spirit fully reignited.

"That's what I'm talking about!" Flint shouted.

"This is the battle I've been waiting for, Johan!"

He gripped his final Poké Ball tightly and tossed it high into the air.

"Let's go, Infernape!"

With an acrobatic flip, a monkey-like Pokémon emerged. Its head was crowned with roaring flames, its body adorned with golden armor-like markings that gleamed in the sunlight.

"Infernape!"

Dragonite instinctively tensed, sensing the overwhelming power radiating from the blazing Pokémon before it.

For most trainers, Flint's team was divided into two categories:

Infernape... and everything else.

Flint's Infernape was a different beast entirely.

Even in official battles, this Infernape had been known to take down two of Cynthia's Pokémon in succession before finally being bested by her Garchomp.

With or without Infernape, Flint was essentially two different trainers.

"Infernape, Power-Up Flame Punch!" Flint roared, wasting no time.

"Infernapppppe!!"

The fire atop its head flared wildly as Infernape dashed forward, its fists igniting in blazing orange flames.

"Extreme Speed!"

Dragonite surged forward, its form blurring as it closed the distance.

CLANG!

Fist met fist with an echoing metallic clash that reverberated through the battlefield. Both Pokémon staggered backward.

"Draa..."

Dragonite shook its hand, exhaling steam. That punch was no joke.

"Infernape!"

Infernape grinned, fire dancing in its eyes as it acknowledged Dragonite as a worthy opponent.

"Feeling the heat, huh?" Flint laughed, throwing his arms wide.

Johan adjusted his glasses, his expression calm but his voice brimming with excitement.

"Let's finish this properly, Dragonite."

The ground cracked under Infernape's feet as it crouched low, its fire blazing higher than ever.

"Flare Blitz!" Flint commanded.

"Not yet. Dragonite, Outrage!" Johan countered.

With a savage roar, Dragonite was enveloped in a crimson aura as it charged, its eyes glowing with a berserk gleam.

The collision was explosive.

BOOM!

When the smoke cleared, both Pokémon stood, battered and breathing heavily—but still standing.

Johan quickly recalled Dragonite before its Outrage-induced confusion could take hold.

"That's enough," he said softly, his tone filled with satisfaction.

Flint's grin stretched even wider, his eyes blazing like his Infernape's flames.

"Now, that's more like it!"

# 336: Flint: "It’s Not Over Yet!" Alolan Ninetales: "No, It’s Over!"

Johan recalled Dragonite and threw out his third Poké Ball.

"Go, Ninetales!"

"Oowoo~~!"

A graceful, snow-white fox leapt elegantly from its Poké Ball, standing tall as it howled toward the sky. In an instant, a fierce snowstorm enveloped the battlefield.

Alolan Ninetales!

"You're using Alolan Ninetales against Infernape?"

Lucian was puzzled by this choice. Flint and Aaron were outright baffled.

Alolan Ninetales was doubly weak to Fire.

And considering the current state of Flint's Infernape—though battered from its exchange with Dragonite—the intensity of its Blaze-driven flames was fiercer than ever.

Even the snowstorm brought by Alolan Ninetales' Snow Warning ability was melting before it could even touch the scorching heat surrounding Infernape.

"With Infernape in this condition... Alolan Ninetales might get one-shotted," Aaron muttered nervously.

Of all of them, Aaron, as a Bug-type specialist, was the most terrified of facing Flint in such a state.

There was simply no solution in his arsenal that could withstand the overwhelming power of Blaze-boosted flames.

"How are you supposed to stop that?"

He didn't know.

"Oowoo~~"

Alolan Ninetales let out another cry, and shimmering Aurora Veils began falling one after another. Layer after layer of protective barriers blanketed the field.

But neither Flint nor his Infernape seemed fazed by Ninetales' presence.

"So, you've got Alolan Ninetales? Perfect!" Flint's eyes sparkled as he raised an arm and pointed dramatically.

"Infernape, Flare Blitz!"

"Kuu!!"

Infernape opened its arms wide as flames roared up from its body. In the blink of an eye, it shot forward like a blazing meteor.

"Aurora Veil? Break it!!"

CRACK! CRACK CRACK!

Under Infernape's relentless charge, the shimmering veils shattered one after another, each one bursting like fragile mirrors.

And yet, Infernape's momentum didn't falter as it closed in on Alolan Ninetales.

Johan's expression remained calm, his voice steady as he gave the next command.

"Ninetales, Blizzard."

"Oowoo~~!"

A brilliant blue light swirled in Ninetales' eyes, and the snowstorm intensified into a raging blizzard.

Empowered by Ice energy and manipulated by psychic power, the ferocious winds began to pierce through Infernape's fiery aura.

Strangely, Alolan Ninetales didn't direct the Blizzard at Infernape.

Instead, the storm swirled tightly around itself, creating a whirling snow vortex centered on its body.

The howling winds and biting cold made it seem almost impossible to approach.

Even so, Infernape pressed forward, its blazing flames melting the snow and ice in its path.

"Now's your chance, Infernape!!" Flint roared.

BOOM!!

With a deafening burst of fire, Infernape's blazing fist slammed into Alolan Ninetales.

CRACK! CRACK CRACK!

Aurora Veils shattered, one after another, until...

THUD!

Infernape's fist stopped inches from Ninetales' body, striking an invisible barrier.

"What the...?"

Everyone was stunned.

"Ice?"

Lucian was the first to notice it—there was a thick, transparent layer of ice surrounding Alolan Ninetales, acting as a protective shield.

Permafrost Barrier.

Within the barrier, Alolan Ninetales' eyes gleamed mischievously, a sly grin playing across its fox-like face.

Johan smirked.

Training battles weren't just for winning—they were for refining techniques that needed honing.

Dragonite had worked on its Controlled Outrage, and now it was Alolan Ninetales' turn with Permafrost Barrier.

No better way to test it than in a high-stakes match.

"Ice, huh?"

Flint blinked in surprise, but his shock didn't last long. He roared, "It's just ice! Infernape, smash it!!"

"Kuu!!"

Infernape's flames surged again, its red-hot fist pressing against the ice as it pushed forward with full force.

CRACK!

The surface of the barrier began to fracture, tiny spiderweb cracks spreading across its icy surface.

BOOM!

With a resounding explosion, the ice shattered into countless fragments.

"Oowoo!"

Alolan Ninetales yelped as Infernape's punch finally landed, sending it skidding back across the snow-covered ground, leaving twin furrows in its wake.

Even Johan had to admit, seeing Flint's Infernape in action was something else entirely.

What kind of overpowered madness was this?

"This thing is insane," Johan muttered under his breath.

He sighed. Battles against these "protagonist-like" trainers were always exhausting.

"Infernape, one more Flare Blitz!" Flint bellowed, now fully in the zone.

But even Johan had his limits.

"You're going for a third Flare Blitz?" Johan muttered, slightly exasperated.

Dragonite had already traded heavy blows with Infernape, and the fiery Pokémon had used Flare Blitz twice.

While powerful, Flare Blitz dealt significant recoil damage to the user. The stronger the hit, the more damage it took in return.

At this rate, how was Infernape still standing?

Was Flint running on some kind of protagonist cheat code?

If this weren't real life, Johan would've sworn Flint had a "no fainting" clause written into his script.

"Well then," Johan muttered, adjusting his strategy.

"Ninetales, Blizzard at full power!"

"Oowoo~~!"

The snowstorm around Ninetales intensified, fueled by icy energy and the howling winds of Mt. Coronet itself.

Flint's Infernape surged forward once again, its blazing flames defying the encroaching cold.

Fire clashed against ice.

The two opposing forces tore through the battlefield, creating an awe-inspiring display of elemental power.

And then—

BOOM BOOM BOOM!!

The fiery charge broke through, flames roaring like an unstoppable avalanche.

But the blizzard was relentless, swirling tighter and tighter around Alolan Ninetales, amplifying its strength until it became an impenetrable vortex.

BOOM!

The clash of ice and fire culminated in a massive explosion, shaking the ground beneath their feet.

When the smoke cleared, an enormous ice sculpture stood at the center of the battlefield.

Inside, Infernape was frozen in place, its flames extinguished, its eyes wide with unyielding determination.

And yet...

The ice began to crack.

"No way..." Aaron whispered.

Lucian adjusted his glasses, his voice calm yet tense. "It's still moving."

But Alolan Ninetales wasn't done.

"Oowoo~~!"

A brilliant blue beam shot from Ninetales' mouth, sealing the cracks and reinforcing the ice around Infernape.

The faint flickers of flame on Infernape's body were completely snuffed out.

Flint stood frozen in place.

"...It's over," Aaron said, breaking the silence.

"No! It's not over!" Flint suddenly shouted. "Infernape, break free!"

The ice sculpture trembled.

Lucian's mouth twitched. "You're kidding me."

But before anyone could react, Ninetales fired another Ice Beam at the frozen Infernape, fortifying the barrier even further.

"..."

Flint opened his mouth to speak, but no words came out.

Ninetales looked back at Johan and tilted its head as if asking, "Should I keep going?"

Johan sighed. "That's enough, Ninetales."

"Oowoo?"

The fox hesitated, its gaze shifting to the massive ice block it had created.

"Are you sure? I could add another layer just in case..."

"No need. Trust me, it's done."

Reluctantly, Ninetales stepped back, letting out a small whine of protest.

Flint, meanwhile, stood motionless, his once-blazing spirit thoroughly frozen.

"Well... that's finally over," Aaron muttered with a sigh of relief.

Lucian adjusted his glasses and gave Johan a complicated look.

As expected of Johan, the ultimate "temperature control specialist."

Whether it was a flickering ember of passion or a blazing inferno, he could extinguish it all with unnerving precision.

This final match between Johan and Flint ended decisively in Johan's favor.

Even so, Johan couldn't help but look back at the frozen Infernape with a faint smile.

For a single Pokémon to push both Dragonite and Ninetales to their limits...

Flint's Infernape was truly a monster.

Suddenly, a familiar voice rang out.

"What's going on here...?"

Johan turned to see Cynthia, freshly returned from her research at the lab.

Her gaze swept across the utterly destroyed battlefield, lingering on the massive block of ice containing Flint's Infernape.

Johan cleared his throat awkwardly and took a step back.

"Uh... Flint did it."

"???"

Flint looked completely dumbfounded.

# 337: Giovanni: Promote Lance and Give Him a Raise! Cynthia’s Home.

"Hahaha, that was a good joke."

Johan chuckled dryly.

Lana might seem quiet and reserved, but she had a playful side.

She loved making exaggerated jokes—like how she once caught Kyogre, a Super Gyarados, or even Lugia while fishing.

She also claimed that the Masked Royal's opponents included Dialga, Palkia, and Darkrai.

Most people, after a brief moment of confusion, could tell she was joking. But some—like her younger sisters and Ash—always took her seriously.

Johan was only laughing dryly because—

Even though he knew it was a joke… she could actually do it.

Lana was in Pastoria for the fishing tournament, accompanied by her father.

Her father was a fisherman—his gear and technique were top-tier, but his luck was abysmal. He could barely catch anything.

Lana's father wasn't a concern.

But Lana herself was absolutely one of the tournament's top contenders.

Seeing that Johan understood her joke, Lana smiled shyly and looked down.

After chatting a bit more—

Lana and Mallow learned that Johan was also participating in the tournament.

But when they found out he didn't have a fishing rod or bait prepared, Lana, as a way to thank him for helping Popplio, immediately offered to lend him some gear.

After finishing their Poké Puffs, the three of them headed to the central hotel in Pastoria City.

Lana showed Johan the fishing gear she had brought.

"Th-This one should do…"

Lana pursed her lips and carefully selected what she considered to be her best and most perfect fishing rod.

It was a collapsible rod with a light blue handle. With a press of a button, the rod extended. Once the line, reel, float, hook, and bait were attached, it was fully assembled.

A very convenient, portable setup.

"Are you sure?"

Johan asked.

It looked like her favorite rod.

But Lana just smiled shyly.

"A true angler… can catch Kyogre even with the simplest rod."

Johan: "…"

She then took out a matching float, reel, and hook from her tackle box.

"Wait!"

Johan's eyes widened when he saw the Misty-shaped fishing hook.

Could this setup be the exact one she used to catch Kyogre?!

Hiss—

Johan felt like he was witnessing—

A golden legend!

A top-tier fishing setup!

"What's wrong?" Lana asked.

"Ahem." Johan cleared his throat. "That… that's Misty."

Lana blushed slightly and muttered, "I'm a fan of hers…"

"When fishing, just attach the bait here," she explained softly, demonstrating the process.

"This is a special bait I made, designed specifically for Sinnoh's waters and fish."

At that moment—

Johan realized.

With this top-tier gear, top-tier bait, and his own fishing skills—

This tournament…

He might actually win!

"Thanks in advance." Johan smiled.

Forget about the rod Anabel's colleague was going to lend him.

A true angler needed top-tier equipment!

—

The next day, the weather was perfect.

The sea was calm, with gentle waves shimmering under the sunlight.

Wingull glided across the sky, while Pelipper dove in and out of the water, splashing in white arcs.

Cynthia stood at the pier, her hair swaying in the breeze. She tucked a few strands behind her ear and smiled confidently.

Pastoria's fishing tournament was about to begin, and Cynthia had brought out her prized fishing gear.

Watching this picturesque scene, Johan couldn't help but feel a little more cheerful.

The tournament had attracted many participants.

Some, like Johan, had decided to join days ago. Others, like Lana, had traveled from different regions. Some had even planned for months in advance.

The prizes were also generous.

First place would receive a top-tier fishing rod co-developed by Devon Corporation and Canalave City, valued at several million Pokédollars!

Second and third place would get a Shell Bell and a Water Stone, respectively.

Even those who didn't place in the top three would receive a high-quality Sitrus Berry set as a consolation prize.

With rewards this appealing, it was no wonder the tournament had so many competitors.

However—

With so many people gathered, there were also hidden dangers.

As the highest-ranking officer present after Anabel, Johan had already spotted a few International Police colleagues discreetly signaling him from the crowd.

Among them—

Was "Handsome," dressed in full fishing gear and looking ready for action.

"Have you gathered all the intel?" Johan asked.

Cynthia smiled warmly.

"Lucian is handling it."

Nearby—

Lucian had set up a sunshade, a chair, and a table.

Dressed in a suit, he sat with his legs crossed, reading a book. A massive pile of books surrounded him.

It looked like he had a lot of research to do.

Johan shook his head slightly.

Ultimate research assistant Lucian—activated.

Next to him, Flint slumped in her chair, covered in bruises.

Apparently—

Yesterday, she had unknowingly wandered into an angler's sacred fishing spot.

Her loud battle challenges and blazing flames had scared away all the fish.

This was basically a declaration of war.

She had barely escaped with her life after nearly being beaten into repentance by every angler there.

So today, she was completely drained.

—

"Aren't insects usually used as bait?"

Aaron, who had spent all day finding a fishing rod, looked at Johan and Cynthia's bait.

Then he looked at his own container full of bugs.

He scratched his head.

Yesterday, he and his Pokémon had spent hours in the forest collecting these.

"Normal insects aren't very effective in these conditions," Johan explained.

Everyone was using bait made from berries mixed with special ingredients to enhance aroma and dispersal.

"Ohhh~~"

Aaron nodded.

Then he glanced at his Dustox with a thoughtful expression.

Dustox: Σ(дlll)

—

"Ka-kaah~!"

Gengar, wearing sunglasses, leaned against Garchomp, whispering in its ear.

The moment we hook a big one, we rush in and pin it down!

Garchomp gave Gengar a side glance.

Do you think too highly of them?

No offense, but Johan and Cynthia look impressive, but they won't catch anything big.

Gengar grinned.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Silly shark, that's where you're wrong.

If there's no big catch…

We'll make one ourselves!

Garchomp suddenly understood.

The two Pokémon exchanged knowing smiles.

—

The tournament officially began!

Many participants immediately set sail.

The tournament offered boat rentals, with different sizes and models available.

The Plus edition fishing boats were said to have a big catch boost—

Faster, sleeker, and—most importantly—more expensive.

Johan had to admire the tournament's marketing strategy.

Slap a "Plus" on it, and suddenly they could charge way more.

And people still rushed to rent them.

These boats allowed anglers to reach prime fishing spots faster, including areas with stronger waves.

And as every seasoned angler knew—

Bigger waves, bigger fish!

Some contestants, however, chose to ride their Pokémon instead.

But that had a downside—

The movement from surfing could scare off rare Water-types.

Lana and Mallow were riding a Lapras.

Others rode Walrein, Swampert, Tentacruel, or even Mantine.

Johan even saw one guy surfing on a Snorlax.

That thing's making waves like a tsunami…

But the most ridiculous sight—

Was someone surfing on a Squirtle.

Johan could tell—

That Squirtle was seconds away from drowning.

"Handsome" also noticed and silently glanced at his Croagunk.

Croagunk:凸(｀△＋）

Johan, Cynthia, and Aaron rented a medium-sized boat and leisurely set off.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar paddled gently, guiding them toward a prime fishing spot.

At the judge's signal—

Countless fishing lines were cast into the water.

Some people quickly reeled in Water-type Pokémon.

The tournament had a point system.

Magikarp was worth 1 point.

More uncommon Pokémon, like Luvdisc, Finneon, and Chinchou, had higher values.

The highest-scoring Pokémon was Milotic.

Johan considered—

Should I catch a Water-type today?

Then—

He felt a tug on his line.

Excited, he reeled it in—

An orange Magikarp flopped onto the boat.

Johan: "…"

Cynthia: pulls up another Magikarp

The two exchanged glances.

Well—

At least neither of them was empty-handed.

—

Meanwhile, beneath the sea—

As the fishing tournament heated up—

A submarine moved silently through the depths.

# 338: Johan: Flint, Eat as Much as You Want

Friday, clear skies.

"Johan-sensei, it's me, Anabel."

A familiar voice came through the phone.

"Anabel? What's up?"

Johan was a bit surprised by the sudden call.

"Johan-sensei, are you in Sinnoh right now?" Instead of answering, Anabel asked a question.

Johan frowned slightly.

What's she getting at?

Although he hadn't deliberately kept his whereabouts a secret, not many people knew he was in Sinnoh. It was clear that some effort had been made to track him down.

So he replied, "You could say that."

Anabel: "…"

What do you mean, you could say that?

"Here's the thing…" Anabel decided to cut to the chase. "We've discovered a group of Pokémon hunters in Sinnoh and are planning to capture them. If you're in Sinnoh, we'd appreciate your help."

Pokémon hunters?

Johan immediately caught the key point.

With Cynthia and the others currently investigating the history of the ancient titans, wasn't this setting up perfectly for a Regigigas awakening scenario?

"Where?" Johan asked, his tone more serious.

If it were anything else, he might not be too concerned.

But when it came to Pokémon hunters… he needed to know the details.

"If our intel is correct, they'll be sailing through Pastoria City. We're planning to set a trap there and catch them as they pass through," Anabel said gravely.

Pastoria City?

Johan thought for a moment and quickly understood why.

Pastoria City was located on Sinnoh's westernmost edge.

The entire city was practically built into the ocean, made up of interconnected islands and bridges.

It was a crucial hub for sea transport between Sinnoh, Kanto, Johto, and Hoenn.

"So, they're trying to smuggle Pokémon out of Sinnoh?"

"Exactly!" Anabel confirmed. "Compared to land routes with multiple checkpoints, the sea allows them to avoid unnecessary trouble."

However…

The sea was also full of dangers.

Some uncharted waters harbored ferocious and powerful wild Pokémon.

Certain sea areas had extreme natural conditions that could easily shatter an ordinary vessel.

Aside from officially recognized and secured routes, the ocean remained a perilous place.

But then there were people like Drake, who constantly sought to challenge and conquer the sea's dangers.

Hearing Anabel's confirmation, Johan's expression turned serious.

After all, his Vulpix had once been smuggled across the ocean to Hoenn.

Anabel added, "Also, with the fishing tournament being held in Pastoria City these days, the crowd and number of boats will make it the perfect cover for them."

Fishing tournament?

For some reason, Johan immediately pictured Feebas getting reeled in.

As a seaside city and a major port, fishing was a popular activity in Pastoria.

"Got it. If the conditions allow, I'll head over," Johan said.

After all, Cynthia had technically hired him, so he couldn't just leave without permission.

"Alright."

With that, Anabel hung up.

"Pastoria City, huh?" Johan put his phone away.

Pokémon hunters operating in Pastoria and using the waterways… this had all the makings of a disaster for them.

"Ka-kaah!"

In the courtyard—

Gengar suddenly shivered and let out a cry.

Upon seeing the figure lurking in the shadows, it patted its chest and let out a deep sigh of relief.

"Ka-kaah~~"

You scared the ghost out of me.

"Marsha!"

Marshadow's expression was pale, looking utterly miserable.

If Gengar weren't hogging Johan's shadow, it wouldn't have had to constantly traverse the Shadow Realm just to keep up with its Chosen One!

It had chased Johan from Johto to Hoenn, then from Hoenn to Sinnoh, from Jubilife City to Canalave City, and now from Canalave to Celestic Town. Was that easy?!

"What's wrong, Gengar?"

Johan stepped outside after finishing his call.

"Marshadow?"

Upon spotting Marshadow, Johan blinked in surprise.

"What are you doing in Sinnoh?"

He hadn't expected Marshadow to follow him all the way here.

"Marsha!"

Seeing Johan, Marshadow straightened its posture.

It looked just as exhausted as Gengar usually did.

Johan chuckled.

"I thought you'd stay in Hoenn. I even asked Gallade to make sure you got fed."

"Marsha… (PД`q.)·.'゜"

Marshadow was devastated.

You could have told me earlier!

Johan pulled out some Pokéblocks.

"Here, eat something to regain your energy."

"Marsha!"

Marshadow turned its head away.

Hmph! I don't need your food!

Johan just laughed.

"Well, we might be leaving Celestic Town soon."

"Marsha?! d(д)"

Johan placed the Pokéblocks on the grass and walked back inside with Gengar.

"Marsha~~"

Marshadow pouted.

Its eyes kept drifting toward the Pokéblocks, the enticing aroma tickling its hungry stomach.

"Marsha…"

Fine, just a little… only so I have the strength to keep watching my Chosen One!

Yeah, that's it!

"You're going to Pastoria too?"

When Johan told Cynthia he might be heading to Pastoria, she mentioned she was going as well.

"Mm…"

Cynthia lazily yawned.

"There are some ancient texts in the Pastoria Library. They don't have digital versions."

The Pastoria Library was Sinnoh's largest.

Not just Sinnoh—even in the entire Pokémon world, it ranked among the top three.

Its collection was vast, covering a wide range of subjects.

There were also many old books detailing Sinnoh's legends.

For Johan, this was great news.

With Cynthia, Lucian, Flint, and Aaron on board, if those Pokémon hunters dared to show up, they'd be instantly wiped out.

Give it a little more time, and even Team Galactic would be instantly wiped out if they showed up.

"I heard…" Aaron lifted his head from the sofa. "Pastoria City is holding a fishing tournament."

He was considering catching a Water-type Bug Pokémon.

At least that way, he wouldn't be at a disadvantage when battling Flint.

"Fishing?"

Cynthia's eyes lit up.

She did enjoy fishing.

She had caught her Milotic alongside Garchomp while fishing.

Seeing Cynthia's excitement, Johan had a thought.

Is Cynthia also a hardcore angler?

"What is that Pokémon?!"

Suddenly—

A startled shout came from the courtyard.

Johan's heart skipped a beat.

He quickly stepped outside—

And saw—

"Marsha?"

Marshadow had its head stuck inside a box.

It struggled for a moment before finally freeing itself, its face full of shock and fear, its mouth covered in Pokéblock crumbs.

"Could this be… the mythical Pokémon Marshadow?"

Lucian, after a brief moment of surprise, spoke in astonishment.

He had read about the Rainbow Hero legend in a book, which mentioned Marshadow as the Shadow Guide.

"Mythical Pokémon?" Flint murmured.

At that moment—

Marshadow finally reacted.

It opened its mouth as if to explain itself, but unable to find the words, it spun around and bolted toward the shadows.

Just before disappearing, it glared at Flint.

"Marsha! |*▼皿▼)"

Then, it vanished into the darkness.

"It ran away?" Flint said, disappointed.

"Marshadow, the Shadow Guide, can enter the Shadow Realm at will," Lucian explained.

Cynthia muttered, "It's actually pretty cute."

C-Cute?

Johan glanced at Flint with sympathy.

Good luck, Flint. Marshadow has marked you.

Best case? You get beaten up. Worst case? You're turned to ashes.

As Lucian explained Marshadow's lore to Flint and Aaron, Cynthia tugged on Johan's sleeve.

"Your Pokémon?" she asked in a low voice.

She had noticed the Pokéblock box on the ground and the crumbs on Marshadow's face.

Clearly, they were Johan's.

Johan shook his head.

"Just… an acquaintance."

With that, the group began preparing to leave for Pastoria.

As Johan passed Flint, he hesitated for a moment before patting her on the shoulder.

"Flint, if there's anything you want to eat… just go ahead and eat a lot of it."

Flint: "???"

# 339: "The God of Fishing"

Cynthia and Lucian went to the Pastoria Library to research.

Johan and Flint, who were somewhat interested in the upcoming fishing tournament, had to prepare—things like fishing rods, bait, and other essentials.

Of course—

Johan hadn't forgotten that he also had an important task: dealing with the Pokémon hunters.

As for Flint—

Being a Fire-type specialist, she had little interest in fishing. And going to the library to read was even less appealing to her.

So after arriving in the city center, she wandered off, probably looking for a battle with another Trainer.

—

Pastoria City's central café.

This café was quite famous throughout Sinnoh, especially for its renowned Poké Puffs.

In a corner of the café—

Johan sat across from Anabel, who was dressed in a sharp black suit.

Anabel handed Johan some documents related to their target.

"The leader of this operation is a Pokémon hunter known as 'Gyarados,' a wanted criminal by the League and our primary target for this mission."

She slid over a photo.

It showed a middle-aged man with a fierce expression, a dark complexion, and a cross-shaped scar on his face.

"They picked their timing well. With the fishing tournament allowing boats to head out to sea, they'll likely blend in with the participants."

She paused before continuing—

"So, I plan to have some of my colleagues enter the tournament undercover."

Johan coughed and finally spoke up.

"In that case, count me in."

"You're entering too?" Anabel looked surprised. "Wait… are you a fishing expert?"

Expert?

"Well, not exactly," Johan tilted his head slightly. "At most, I'm just a passing fisherman."

Regardless—

Fishermen never go home empty-handed!

"I see." Anabel nodded.

With Johan participating, those Pokémon hunters wouldn't be escaping through the tournament.

"That said, I'll need a favor from your department for the tournament." Johan added.

Anabel became more serious.

"Go ahead. If it's within reason, we'll provide full support!"

As long as he wasn't asking for a hundred officers, it was fine.

The International Police were always short on manpower.

"Uh… can you lend me a fishing rod?"

Anabel: "?"

"Preferably with some pre-mixed bait," Johan added.

Wait—

You're a fisherman… without a rod?

"I— I understand. Handsome probably brought extra. I'll borrow one for you."

Even "Handsome" was here?

It seemed the International Police were taking this mission very seriously.

After a bit more discussion, Anabel left to handle other matters.

Johan, on the other hand, stayed to enjoy the café's famous Poké Puffs with his Pokémon.

"Ka-kaah~!"

Gengar rolled up its tongue and devoured the Poké Puff in one gulp.

"Oowoo~~"

Ninetales was also enjoying them one bite at a time.

"The taste is really good," Johan commented after trying one himself.

"We should bring some back for Gallade and the others. With Gallade's cooking skills, it might even be able to replicate them."

—

Just as they were eating, a commotion broke out outside.

"Popplio!"

Hmm?

Johan instinctively turned toward the window.

A flustered Popplio was darting through the crowd, eyes filled with fear. It occasionally blew bubbles from its nose, encapsulating pedestrians and their Pokémon.

"Popplio, the Sea Lion Pokémon. A pure Water-type. It tirelessly practices creating large bubbles, Rotom!"

Rotom Pokédex briefly lifted its head from a slice of cake and provided the description.

This was one of Alola's starter Pokémon.

Its final evolution, Primarina, was known for having one of the most beautiful singing voices in the Pokémon world.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar scratched its head.

That little guy seems really scared.

Right then—

The panicked Popplio dashed straight toward the café.

"Ninetales," Johan called softly, standing up and moving toward Popplio.

"Pop?"

The frightened Popplio suddenly noticed Johan, as well as his Ninetales and Gengar beside him.

Its anxiety only increased, and it reflexively blew out another large bubble from its nose.

Johan's vision flickered with Popplio's Pokédex entry.

A quick scan, and he immediately understood why it was so afraid.

It had once been abused by a group of cruel individuals. Although it had been rescued, the trauma left it unable to trust Trainers, fearing humans in general.

Johan sighed.

Before Popplio's bubbles could reach him, Gengar casually caught them.

"Ka-kaah?"

The round, purple ghost toyed with the bubbles, finding them rather amusing.

"Pop? Popplio?"

Popplio froze in confusion.

"Popplio," Johan crouched down, smiling gently at it.

He released a subtle wave of Aura—

A warm, reassuring presence spread outward, gradually calming Popplio's nerves.

"Popplio!"

Meanwhile—

Lana was pushing through the crowd, anxiously searching for Popplio.

She had rescued this Popplio from Team Skull not long ago, but its past trauma made it hard for it to trust anyone.

Today, Mallow had invited her to try Pastoria's famous Poké Puffs.

She thought it would be a good idea to let Popplio taste them as well.

But when a group of people approached them, Popplio panicked and ran away.

"Popplio!"

Mallow cupped her hands around her mouth and called out.

After a bit more pushing through the crowd—

Lana finally spotted Popplio.

But when she saw what it was doing, she froze.

Mallow, who had caught up, also stood in stunned silence.

—

The once-panicked Popplio—

Was now shyly taking a Poké Puff from the hand of a handsome young man with a gentle smile.

"Take your time. There's more if you want," the young man said, his voice warm as he lightly patted Popplio's head.

"Popplio!"

The delicious Poké Puff made Popplio clap its fins happily, eyes forming crescent moons.

"That's…" Mallow was shocked.

It was her first time seeing Popplio so comfortable around a stranger.

Even Lana, its Trainer, had never seen it act this way.

At that moment—

Popplio noticed Lana and Mallow approaching.

It hesitated, still afraid of the people in the crowd, but eventually waddled toward them.

"Popplio!" Lana immediately scooped it up, finally breathing a sigh of relief.

"Pop! Pop!"

The surrounding crowd, realizing there was no more commotion, gradually dispersed.

"You're… Johan-sensei?"

Mallow rubbed her eyes, recognizing the young man.

Actually—

Johan had recognized them first.

Two of Ash's future classmates at the Alola Pokémon School.

And future Trial Captains—Lana, the Water-type Captain, and Mallow, the Grass-type Captain.

Johan had a particularly strong impression of Lana.

Because of her—

Feebas' reputation had plummeted.

But also because of her—

The world where Groudon suffered alone never came to be.

Since they were rivals, it had to be fair—

Groudon couldn't fly, and Kyogre had to be fished up!

This girl—

She was undoubtedly… The God of Fishing!

As for Mallow—

Johan also had an impression of her, mostly because her family ran a restaurant, and she was a great cook.

However—

She had a habit of experimenting with new ingredients, sometimes adding completely bizarre elements—like Thunderbolt-flavored dishes.

A true master of cursed cuisine.

Alola Pokémon School was full of hidden talents.

"Johan-sensei?"

Hearing Mallow's surprised tone, Lana also looked up and recognized him.

Johan smiled.

"You know me?"

They both nodded in unison.

The ever-enthusiastic Mallow explained,

"Professor Kukui often shows us your battle videos as training material!"

"Professor Kukui, huh…" Johan nodded.

The same guy who once invited him to Alola as a guest teacher—The Masked Royal.

Though—

Johan's expression became slightly strange.

Using his battles as study material for kids…

Was that really okay?

Yesterday, she had unknowingly wandered into an angler's sacred fishing spot.

Her loud battle challenges and blazing flames had scared away all the fish.

This was basically a declaration of war.

She had barely escaped with her life after nearly being beaten into repentance by every angler there.

So today, she was completely drained.

—

"Aren't insects usually used as bait?"

Aaron, who had spent all day finding a fishing rod, looked at Johan and Cynthia's bait.

Then he looked at his own container full of bugs.

He scratched his head.

Yesterday, he and his Pokémon had spent hours in the forest collecting these.

"Normal insects aren't very effective in these conditions," Johan explained.

Everyone was using bait made from berries mixed with special ingredients to enhance aroma and dispersal.

"Ohhh~~"

Aaron nodded.

Then he glanced at his Dustox with a thoughtful expression.

Dustox: Σ(дlll)

—

"Ka-kaah~!"

Gengar, wearing sunglasses, leaned against Garchomp, whispering in its ear.

The moment we hook a big one, we rush in and pin it down!

Garchomp gave Gengar a side glance.

Do you think too highly of them?

No offense, but Johan and Cynthia look impressive, but they won't catch anything big.

Gengar grinned.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Silly shark, that's where you're wrong.

If there's no big catch…

We'll make one ourselves!

Garchomp suddenly understood.

The two Pokémon exchanged knowing smiles.

—

The tournament officially began!

Many participants immediately set sail.

The tournament offered boat rentals, with different sizes and models available.

The Plus edition fishing boats were said to have a big catch boost—

Faster, sleeker, and—most importantly—more expensive.

Johan had to admire the tournament's marketing strategy.

Slap a "Plus" on it, and suddenly they could charge way more.

And people still rushed to rent them.

These boats allowed anglers to reach prime fishing spots faster, including areas with stronger waves.

And as every seasoned angler knew—

Bigger waves, bigger fish!

Some contestants, however, chose to ride their Pokémon instead.

But that had a downside—

The movement from surfing could scare off rare Water-types.

Lana and Mallow were riding a Lapras.

Others rode Walrein, Swampert, Tentacruel, or even Mantine.

Johan even saw one guy surfing on a Snorlax.

That thing's making waves like a tsunami…

But the most ridiculous sight—

Was someone surfing on a Squirtle.

Johan could tell—

That Squirtle was seconds away from drowning.

"Handsome" also noticed and silently glanced at his Croagunk.

Croagunk:凸(｀△＋）

Johan, Cynthia, and Aaron rented a medium-sized boat and leisurely set off.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar paddled gently, guiding them toward a prime fishing spot.

At the judge's signal—

Countless fishing lines were cast into the water.

Some people quickly reeled in Water-type Pokémon.

The tournament had a point system.

Magikarp was worth 1 point.

More uncommon Pokémon, like Luvdisc, Finneon, and Chinchou, had higher values.

The highest-scoring Pokémon was Milotic.

Johan considered—

Should I catch a Water-type today?

Then—

He felt a tug on his line.

Excited, he reeled it in—

An orange Magikarp flopped onto the boat.

Johan: "…"

Cynthia: pulls up another Magikarp

The two exchanged glances.

Well—

At least neither of them was empty-handed.

—

Meanwhile, beneath the sea—

As the fishing tournament heated up—

A submarine moved silently through the depths.

# 341: The One Fishing... Is Gengar?!

"This doesn't make sense…"

Johan turned to look at the pile of flopping Magikarp on the boat, along with a few Shellder and Goldeen.

No need to guess—other than the Magikarp, the rest were caught by Cynthia and Aaron.

"Top-tier golden fishing gear, top-tier bait… Did I sign some sort of contract with Magikarp? Something must be off!"

But for now, Johan couldn't figure out what the problem was.

At the top of the leaderboard was Lana.

She had already reeled in two Chinchou, a Luvdisc, two Starmie, and a bunch of other high-scoring Water-types, not to mention an absurd number of lower-point Pokémon.

"Forget it. I give up."

Since he couldn't figure it out, Johan decided to just go with the flow.

At least he wasn't going home empty-handed.

Besides, his real goal today wasn't fishing.

"Marsha?"

From the shadow of the boat, Marshadow emerged, gazing at the pile of flopping Magikarp with a thoughtful look.

Are we making Magikarp stew tonight?

Magikarp stew… didn't sound bad.

But it had to be the biggest and fattest one!

Marshadow's eyes swept over the pile of Magikarp—

Before locking onto the largest and plumpest one.

Slurp—

Marshadow swallowed its saliva.

And the Magikarp, sensing its gaze, shivered.

Magikarp: (_.)

SPLASH!

It instantly used Splash Attack, sending water splattering all over Marshadow's face.

"Ka-kaah~! (*σ´∀｀)"

Gengar pointed at Marshadow and burst into uncontrollable laughter.

"Marsha! (◣_◢)"

Marshadow's eyes sharpened.

Magikarp froze in fear.

The next moment—

A dazzling white light burst from its body!

Marshadow blinked in confusion.

Johan and the others also turned their heads, drawn to the sudden glow.

"Wait… did it just…?" Aaron wobbled his ahoge.

"It evolved?" Cynthia said with slight surprise.

Johan instinctively looked at Gengar.

"Ka-kaah! Ka-kaah!"

Gengar waved its hands frantically.

Not me! I swear! I didn't even touch it! It was Marshadow!

Without hesitation, Gengar sold Marshadow out.

Johan turned to the corner of the boat—

Marshadow had just realized what happened. Seeing Johan's gaze, it panicked, turned around, and dove back into the shadows.

Johan's eye twitched.

Why does this always happen around me?

"RAAAARRGGHH!!"

The expanding white light was followed by a deafening roar!

"It's a gigantic Gyarados!"

The announcer finally took notice of their boat.

A normal Gyarados was about 6.5 meters long—

But this one, originally a massive Magikarp, had grown to over 7.5 meters after evolving!

Gyarados was already a high-scoring Pokémon. With its size bonus—

"RAAAAARGH!! GLUB GLUB—"

SPLOOSH!

Johan's little fishing boat couldn't handle a Gyarados that huge—

So Garchomp immediately yeeted it back into the ocean.

When Gyarados surfaced again, it looked… confused.

It turned and saw Garchomp's expressionless face—

And Gengar's menacing grin as it cracked its knuckles.

…

This was not how it imagined its evolution going.

"Ka-kaah~~"

I'm happy for your evolution, but I didn't like your attitude earlier.

Gengar put on its swim goggles—

And jumped in first!

Blub blub blub—

Garchomp snorted.

A dragon like me has no business in the water.

But before Gengar could reach Gyarados—

It suddenly popped back up, eyes gleaming with excitement.

"Ka-kaah!!"

BIG TOOTHY FISH!!

It gestured wildly, puffing up its cheeks in an exaggerated motion.

Toothy fish?

Johan blinked.

How big?

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar pointed at Gyarados.

BIGGER THAN THAT!

Bigger than a 7.5-meter-long Gyarados?

Was it Kyogre?!

Johan glanced toward Lana's direction—

The water was calm.

…So probably not.

Or maybe—

Team Rocket's submarine?

But weren't they still busy filming a movie?

And let's be real—Team Rocket's taste in submarines leaned more toward Magikarp or Gyarados, not Sharpedo.

Suddenly—

Johan pulled out his phone and called Anabel.

"Johan-sensei?" Anabel answered.

"Anabel, do we have surveillance underwater?" Johan asked.

"Underwater?" Anabel sounded confused.

Her hesitation confirmed it—

The International Police hadn't set up any underwater monitoring. They were only watching the boats on the surface.

"What's going on?"

Cynthia noticed Johan's change in expression and asked.

"I think… we've found the Pokémon hunter gang," Johan said gravely.

"Pokémon hunters?"

Cynthia's eyes widened slightly before her expression grew serious.

"Gengar," Johan called out.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar floated on the surface.

"See if you can drag them out!"

Gengar turned to the dazed Gyarados—

And grinned wickedly.

"Ka-kaah…"

It vanished into a cloud of black mist, reappearing on Gyarados's head.

It patted Gyarados's crest.

Behave and take me down there—

Or else…

"RAAAAAAARGH!!"

Gyarados roared in fury and prepared to lash out.

BZZZZT—

Electricity crackled ominously from Gengar's palm—

Dancing right in front of Gyarados's eyes.

Gengar's twisted grin reflected in its pupils—

Amidst the flickering blue lightning.

Or else I'll fry you.

"…RAAAARRGHH!!"

Gyarados immediately surrendered.

Every Pokémon had a duty to capture criminals!

It flipped over and darted toward the target, with Gengar proudly surfing on its back—

Like a ghostly warrior riding the waves!

"Milotic, Gastrodon—go help!"

Cynthia tossed two Poké Balls.

Her Pokémon dove in and followed after Gengar and Gyarados.

At the same time—

The International Police officers all received Anabel's urgent message in their earpieces.

"All units, we've found the target! Keep your eyes on Gengar—Johan-sensei will bring them out!"

Handsome, who was rowing a boat, immediately straightened up.

So the hunters really were sneaking through underwater…

He had suspected it earlier—

But lacked the means to confirm it.

He turned to his Croagunk.

Croagunk: 凸(｀△＋）

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar shouted.

FISHING ROD!

Ghostly surfer Gengar zoomed past—

Croagunk grabbed Handsome's fishing rod and threw it toward Gengar without hesitation.

Then—

With an evil grin, Gengar whipped the rod forward!

The hook latched onto the giant Sharpedo.

"Ka-kaah!!"

GET OVER HERE!!

Sharpedo started surfacing—

And the Pokémon hunters realized—

They had been found.

—

Inside a massive submarine shaped like a Sharpedo—

"Boss, something's latched onto the hull!"

The leader of the Pokémon hunters smirked.

"Who cares? Just ram your way through!"

"Full speed ahead!"

The sudden movement inevitably drew the tournament's attention.

"T-That's a giant Sharpedo!!"

"One contestant is locked in a battle with it—it's… it's…?"

When the announcer finally saw who was reeling in the Sharpedo—

They froze.

"The one fishing is… is… GENGAR?!"

The entire audience erupted.

Meanwhile—

Johan rode his Dragonite, Cynthia crouched on Garchomp, her golden hair whipping in the wind—

And Aaron surfed in on his Masquerain.

"Let's lure them out of the tournament area first," Johan ordered.

"Understood!"

The International Police all responded in unison.

The hunt had begun.

# 342: Johan-Sensei’s True Colors Revealed!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

"Seismitoad, use Mud Shot!"

A rough, bellowing voice rang out from the Sharpedo submarine.

Moments later, clumps of mud shot straight toward Ninetales.

At the same time—

BOOM! BOOM! BOOM!

The Sharpedo submarine rapidly launched several small submersibles, scattering in different directions to escape.

"Oowoo~~"

A haunting, ethereal cry echoed through the air.

Snowflakes began to swirl as a biting blizzard howled across the ocean's surface.

Ice crystals spread across the water, and the incoming Mud Shots turned into frozen clumps, crashing into the ice with dull thuds.

The howling blizzard threatened to freeze everything in its path.

"S-So strong…"

A member of the International Police, riding a water scooter toward the battlefield, gulped nervously.

He realized he could abandon the scooter and just walk on the frozen sea.

In his earpiece—

Johan's voice came through.

"Close the net!"

"Yes, sir!"

The officer instinctively straightened his back and saluted, even though Johan couldn't see him.

It wasn't just the International Police—

The Pastoria City Police, led by Officer Jenny, had also arrived from all directions, surrounding the Pokémon hunters.

However—

The hunters were strong.

Not all of them had been frozen by Ninetales. Some were still fighting back.

And—

Not all of those escaping in the mini-submarines had been stopped.

"Dammit! They're getting away! Eight o'clock direction!"

"Twelve o'clock direction!"

"Three o'clock direction!"

Clearly—

Those escaping via mini-subs were likely the key figures of the Pokémon hunter syndicate.

"I'll handle the eight o'clock group," Anabel said, pressing her earpiece while riding Metagross.

"Metagross!!"

With a flash of blue light, Metagross extended its psychic energy toward the fleeing submersible.

At twelve o'clock—

Aaron, dressed in a deep red suit, stood atop his Bronzong with a helpless sigh.

"Bronzong, Trick Room!"

Hummm—

A spatial distortion spread outward.

Aaron adjusted his glasses, sighing again.

"I just came to read some books… How did I get roped into this?"

More importantly—

How do you say no to Johan-sensei's request?

Damn it, Flint. I won't forget this grudge!

"Bronzong, Psychic!"

Meanwhile, Flint—

Was still rowing a tiny fishing boat toward the fight.

At three o'clock—

"Finally, our turn!" Aaron grinned and tossed out a Poké Ball.

A gleaming red Scizor landed on the frozen battlefield.

But as soon as the battle began—

Aaron's expression shifted entirely.

His voice became sharp and commanding:

"Scizor, Close Combat!"

—

Johan clicked his tongue in admiration.

"Wow… Reliable, aren't they?"

Seeing Sinnoh's three future Elite Four members and its future Champion all engaged in battle—

Johan couldn't help but feel sorry for the Pokémon hunters.

Even if they got thrown into jail, this would be a story they could brag about.

"Yeah, back in my day, it took Sinnoh's entire Elite Four and Champion to take me down."

Instant street cred in prison.

However—

Even the tightest net has holes.

"Four o'clock! Fast-moving target!!"

A mini-sub was intercepted—

But instead of stopping, it self-destructed!

A massive Sharpedo burst from the water.

And riding its back—

"HAHAHAHA!! You think you can catch me?! Not in this lifetime!"

A brawny, scarred man leapt onto the Sharpedo's back.

With a burst of Aqua Jet, he shot forward, rapidly gaining speed.

It was an Accelerate Sharpedo!

And—

He was the leader of this Pokémon hunter syndicate!

Johan frowned.

That shouldn't be possible. There should be someone else…

Then—

He realized.

Flint!

He was still rowing her tiny boat toward them.

Because they had tried to avoid causing too much of a disturbance, they had let the Sharpedo sub get too far out.

And these Pokémon hunters—

Had zero team spirit.

The moment things went south, they all ran for their lives.

Cynthia noticed the situation and called over Garchomp.

"I'll handle it," Johan said, taking a deep breath.

An Accelerate Sharpedo…

Garchomp might struggle to keep up in the short term.

And if Sharpedo dove underwater, Cynthia would be at a disadvantage.

Cynthia looked at Johan for a moment before pressing her lips together and nodding.

"Be careful."

She swiftly withdrew Milotic and shoved the Poké Ball into Johan's hand.

"It'll help you."

Johan nodded, accepting the Poké Ball without argument.

Then, with a swift motion, he recalled his Pokémon and leapt onto Dragonite's back.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar whacked Gyarados on the head before flying over to Johan.

Alright, big guy! I'm leaving you in charge—I'll be back!

"RAAAARRGH!!"

Go get 'em, boss!

Johan turned to Cynthia.

"I'm leaving this to you—stay safe."

Cynthia simply tied her hair up, flashing a confident smile.

"Don't worry."

Johan suddenly felt bad for the Pokémon hunters.

He remembered—

How Cynthia had single-handedly wiped out an entire army.

Leaving her here…

Was just giving her an excuse to go all out.

Johan patted Dragonite, tightened his grip, and took a deep breath.

"Dragonite, Dragon Dance! Extreme Speed!"

…Would he survive this?

After all—

According to Professor Oak, Dragonite could circumnavigate the Earth in 16 hours.

"Oowoo~~"

Dragonite's eyes sparkled with excitement.

Finally! A chance to show Johan real speed!!

With an explosive flap of its wings—

Dragonite shot forward, its massive body barely slowing it down.

Garchomp, watching from below—

Cracked its knuckles.

A low, metallic grinding sound echoed through the air.

Its expression…

Was somewhere between rage and pure battle lust.

Oh? Another one dancing in front of me?

Johan barely heard Cynthia's command before—

The screams of Pokémon hunters echoed from below.

They danced in front of Garchomp…

They paid the price.

—

With Dragon Dance, Agility, and Extreme Speed stacked, Dragonite tore across the ocean, leaving massive waves in its wake.

Johan's grip tightened.

For a moment—

He forgot to be scared of the speed.

Pokémon hunters were—

The purest embodiment of evil in this world.

They captured, sold, and abused Pokémon for profit.

Johan had spent years treating Pokémon with psychological trauma, and many cases had been caused by hunters like these.

The more he learned—

The more he hated them.

This wasn't just about Vulpix and Indeedee.

This was for every Pokémon who had ever suffered at their hands.

"If I let you escape—"

"I will never forgive myself!"

—

Meanwhile, the Pokémon hunter leader laughed wildly.

"HAHAHAHA!!"

"You think you can catch me?! IDIOTS!"

By having his men scatter, he had torn a hole in the net.

"This capture attempt is a joke!"

Holding onto Sharpedo's dorsal fin, he kept accelerating.

But then—

He felt it.

A chilling, murderous intent creeping up behind him.

He whipped his head around—

His pupils shrinking in terror.

"IMPOSSIBLE!!"

Johan was already right behind him.

Dragonite smirked.

Where do you think you're going?!

"Dragonite—Thunder!"

BZZZZZZZZZT!!

A golden bolt of lightning slammed into Sharpedo and its rider.

"SHIT!!"

The hunter leader screamed as electricity surged through him.

He barely had time to recover before—

Red light flashed.

Ninetales landed on the frozen ocean.

Ice spread across the water.

And one by one—

Johan's Pokémon surrounded him.

Gengar, Tinkaton, Kubfu, Charcadet, Milotic…

Johan stepped forward, adjusting his glasses.

"This area… is a no-escape zone."

The hunter leader shuddered.

Johan-sensei's true colors…

Had been exposed.
