# 343: Matori—The Gift Is Ready!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

"No problem, right?"

On a seaside island near Canalave City, Officer Jenny glanced at her watch.

Johan had been chasing for quite some time now.

"It'll be fine," Cynthia said softly, her gaze drifting toward the ocean.

"With Johan's strength, ordinary Pokémon hunters wouldn't even get a chance to fight back. This one shouldn't be much of a problem either," Anabel added.

All the other Pokémon hunters had already been apprehended, leaving only the last major target.

"He's coming."

Looker closed his book, adjusted his glasses, and spoke.

They looked up—

On the water's surface, a path of ice slowly extended outward. Johan, leading with his Alolan Ninetales and Gengar, walked steadily along the frozen path. Behind him, the Pokémon hunter leader trailed lifelessly, drooling slightly from his slackened mouth.

"He seems… very cooperative?" Aaron remarked in surprise.

Looker shot him a glance.

Cooperative?

More like his soul had been drained!

Johan, walking on the ice, muttered to himself:

"I really should get a bike…"

Riding a bike on water—how cool would that be?

Wherever he pedaled, the ice path would extend.

Much flashier than flying on a Dragonite.

And safer, too.

"Speaking of which, they should have Rotom Bikes by now, right?"

"Ro…Rotom?"

Rotom froze for a moment.

Wait—was he about to ride me?

As Johan neared the shore, he noticed three women waiting expectantly. He quickly straightened his expression.

When there are three women together, trouble is bound to follow. Better tread carefully.

The ice path extended to the shore.

Before Anabel or the International Police could react, the hunter leader suddenly shivered, then burst into tears as he lunged at one of the officers.

"Quick! Arrest me! Arrest me now!"

A collective question mark hovered over everyone's heads.

They exchanged bewildered glances.

What… just happened?

Johan chuckled dryly. "Well, at least he's got a good attitude about it."

Good attitude?

Was that the issue here?

"No injuries?" Cynthia asked.

Johan shook his head. "No injuries."

As he spoke, he returned the Poké Ball containing Milotic to Cynthia.

"Oh, right." He turned to Anabel. "He knows some things about other Pokémon hunter groups—"

Before he could finish, the hunter leader blurted out,

"I'll confess! I'll confess to everything!"

Anabel gave Johan an odd look.

Maybe he was better suited for interrogation work?

She had never seen a criminal so eager to cooperate.

Officer Jenny had the same thought.

In fact, Johan seemed like he'd fit in well with their family—great at tactical planning, helpful in investigations, and even had interrogation skills.

Wasn't this the perfect "Jenny Son-in-Law" template?

Feeling something was off, Johan quickly changed the subject:

"Alright, let's go. We've still got a competition to finish."

With that, he took Cynthia and Aaron back to the fishing tournament, leaving Anabel and Officer Jenny to handle the situation.

They, in turn, decided to report everything truthfully—Johan would surely receive a nice reward.

—

On the way back.

Johan suddenly frowned.

"Are we forgetting something?"

Meanwhile—

Flint was still rowing toward the island.

"Let's congratulate the winner of this year's fishing tournament—Lana from Alola!"

In the end, the champion of the tournament was Lana.

No surprise there.

She even caught a wild Milotic in the final round, earning the legendary fishing set as her prize.

Johan, on the other hand, managed to take second place thanks to a massive Gyarados and some "connections," winning the Shell Bell item.

[Shell Bell: When a Pokémon holding this item successfully lands an attack, it restores HP equal to 1/8th of the damage dealt.]

In short, a lifesteal item.

Johan thought for a moment.

Any of his Pokémon could use it, but the one it suited best…

Probably Kubfu.

The little guy hadn't gotten a new "toy" in a while.

And the Shell Bell fit perfectly with both Kubfu's single-strike and multi-strike techniques.

"If I equip eight Shell Bells, does that mean I can achieve perfect lifesteal?"

—

After the award ceremony.

Johan returned the Kyogre Fishing Rod to Lana.

Lana and Mallow then invited him to visit Alola.

"If you come to Alola, stop by my family's Aina Kitchen! I'll treat you to a meal!" Mallow said enthusiastically.

She had no idea what kind of massive expense that invitation might bring—especially if Gengar came along.

"I-I…"

Shy and soft-spoken, Lana hesitated for a long time before finally squeezing out:

"I'll show you my technique for fishing up Kyogre!"

She was completely serious.

Johan: "…"

Please, keep your divine fishing skills to yourself.

Somewhere, Archie was probably crying in a corner.

—

Back at Looker's mansion, Johan got a call.

"Hey, Johan! Where are you guys?"

Huh?

Johan checked the caller ID.

Oh.

So that's what he'd forgotten—Flint!

"Ahem, we changed locations. I'll send you the coordinates."

"Awesome! Leave the strongest one for me!"

Before Flint could finish, Johan hung up and sent the location.

Out at sea, rowing his small boat, Flint stared at the GPS coordinates.

Something felt off.

"…Wait, isn't this Looker's place?"

—

Rocket HQ, Viridian City.

"Boss, we're heading to Sinnoh."

Matori's expression was a bit downcast as she spoke to Giovanni through the large screen.

Giovanni swirled the red wine in his glass indifferently.

"…Boss, while I'm away, please take care of yourself. Don't forget to eat on time."

Giovanni: "…"

Meowth: Are you trying to steal my job?!

The feline Pokémon shot Matori a hostile glare.

C'mon, is she trying to replace me?!

"Ahem." Giovanni patted Meowth's back and asked, "You're stationed in Sinnoh now?"

"No, just a seven-day business trip."

Then why do you sound like you're saying goodbye forever?

"I heard Johan's in Sinnoh too?" Giovanni changed the subject.

"Yes!"

Matori's energy instantly returned.

Seeing Johan again was always good news—exciting!

"Oh, right."

Matori suddenly remembered something.

"Lately, some people have been looking into Johan's identity again."

"The usual," Giovanni waved dismissively.

"Understood!"

Matori nodded.

Rocket's latest operation had wiped out the "Collectors" group. With their newly acquired mining resources, they were about to open a new excavation site. A little extra manpower wouldn't hurt.

"…And Johan's year-end bonus and gift—are they ready?" Giovanni asked.

"All set!"

Matori beamed.

"No way Johan will be disappointed!"

After all, the gift was personally selected by Giovanni himself.

No problems there.

Giovanni nodded.

"Go, then. And be quick."

"Yes, sir!! ((//Д//))"

—

That night.

Johan learned from Cynthia that their next destination was Iron Island.

Located off Sinnoh's western coast, Iron Island was once rich in mineral resources like Oreburgh City.

However, over-mining had left it depleted.

To protect the wild Pokémon living there, most of the island had been closed off. Only one ferry route—Canalave City to Iron Island—remained open.

"There's an ancient site there—Iron Ruins," Cynthia said.

Iron Ruins?

Johan vaguely recalled something from the games…

Wasn't Registeel supposed to be sleeping there?

In reality, that probably wasn't the case.

But Iron Ruins did exist.

Also…

Canalave City's Gym Leader, Byron, was said to be training on Iron Island.

Yet, he hadn't shown up during the Pokémon hunter incident.

That was definitely going to get him written up.

—

The next morning.

Johan and the others boarded a ferry to Iron Island.

As they sailed, Looker recounted the events of the previous day to Flint.

Flint, devastated, lamented:

"…How did I miss out on such an epic moment?!"

# 344: Iron Island—Riley and Byron (BONUS)

Sinnoh, Iron Island.

Gengar tearfully bid farewell to Gyarados.

"Ka-kaah~~ o(TヘTo)"

Goodbye, my dear ride.

"Garrr~~"

Gyarados also shed tears of relief.

Finally free!

This was too much! Life was too hard!

Seeing Gyarados cry, Gengar wiped its eyes and couldn't help but suggest:

"Ka-kaah?"

How about I just catch you in a Poké Ball?

A Pokémon catching a Pokémon?

Gyarados froze for a moment at the suggestion before diving deep into the water.

Run! Fast!

It splashed Gengar with water in the process.

So cheeky.

Gengar wiped its face.

"To enter the Iron Ruins, we need to meet someone first," Looker said as he led Johan and the others further onto the island.

"Cha!"

The Poké Ball on Johan's waist suddenly popped open, and his Tinkaton, hammer in hand, appeared beside him.

Her hammer buzzed like a scanner.

Metal detected!

And possibly valuable metal at that!

Johan quickly pulled the little one back.

That's private property!

That's the Pokémon Center!

Meanwhile…

Inside a house on Iron Island, a young man dressed in a suit and top hat, exuding a gentlemanly air, suddenly opened his eyes.

Beside him, a Lucario meditating with closed eyes did the same.

Man and Pokémon locked eyes, both seeing the same hint of surprise.

Aura!

It was the presence of another Aura Guardian.

And not just any Aura—it was pure, as if it came from the very source of Aura itself!

So, he wasn't the only one in this world!

Riley looked at Lucario, a rare excitement on his face, and spoke in a deep voice:

"Lucario, I am not alone in my path!"

"Luca!"

Lucario nodded firmly.

There was something else it wanted to say.

That presence…

It felt even purer and more familiar than Riley's own Aura.

If anything, Riley might actually be the junior one here.

Even though the newcomer's Aura wasn't particularly strong yet.

"Let's go, Lucario. Time to welcome our guest."

Riley stood up and opened the courtyard gate just as Johan and the others arrived.

Johan narrowed his eyes slightly before realizing—

Iron Island's Riley?

The last known Aura Guardian?

Suddenly, he understood why Looker brought them here.

Iron Island, large as it was…

It was Riley's private property!

Not only was he a mining tycoon, but he was also the island's owner.

To enter the sealed mining tunnels that led to the Iron Ruins, they needed his permission.

And Riley wasn't just anyone—he was a descendant of Sir Aaron from Cameran Palace, inheriting his Aura.

In a way…

Johan and Riley's Aura abilities shared the same lineage.

After all, Johan's Aura came from Aaron's disciple, Lucario, and from Mew—or rather, the Tree of Beginning.

While Riley's Aura came directly from Aaron himself.

The question was—

How much of Aaron's power had Riley inherited? Could he throw Aura Spheres by hand?

"I am Riley. Welcome to Iron Island."

Riley tipped his hat with a smile.

"Mr. Riley, we are…"

Looker and Riley had some prior acquaintance, so after a brief introduction, he explained their purpose.

"Investigating the ancient giants?"

Riley appeared thoughtful.

But he didn't refuse Looker's request. Instead, he readily agreed:

"Of course, no problem."

He then led the group toward the central mountain of the island—the entrance to the Iron Ruins, and once one of the mining tunnels.

Along the way…

Riley realized he had underestimated Johan.

Johan's Aura wasn't just purer than his—it carried a sacred presence that commanded reverence.

However, Johan showed no intention of revealing that he possessed Aura at all.

So, Riley remained silent on the matter.

After all, he himself rarely disclosed his Aura abilities unless necessary.

Except…

Why was that sacred aura growing stronger?

It was almost like…

At the back of the group, Johan's expression twitched.

…Hungry again?

He discreetly channeled some Aura into the Rainbow Feather.

He wasn't sure if it was just his imagination, but…

Under his Aura's infusion, the Rainbow Feather seemed to be growing more alive.

…Surely it wouldn't hatch into a baby Ho-Oh, right?

If it did—

Who would be considered the parent?

Ho-Oh?

Or him?

Johan shook his head at the thought.

It was just a feather—no way it could be that powerful.

Once he sensed the feather was "full," he stopped.

But up ahead, Riley was shaken.

That sacred aura was so in tune with Johan—it even seemed affectionate toward him!

This man was undoubtedly a brave and righteous Aura Guardian!

Meanwhile, hidden in the shadows far behind them…

A small Marshadow leaned on a wooden stick, quietly observing Johan.

It had long since grown used to the Rainbow Feather's presence.

In fact…

"Marsha…"

That Aura of his…

What did it taste like?

Even Marshadow was tempted to try it.

—

"Please wait a moment. I'll check if the tunnel is still safe."

Near the summit, Riley spoke.

Since the tunnels hadn't been used in ages, their condition was uncertain. To ensure safety, he decided to inspect them first.

"Much appreciated," Looker said.

The group took a seat on nearby stone benches.

"…So, how strong is this Riley guy?" Drake asked the question that interested him most.

"Not weak."

Before Looker could answer, Cynthia spoke first.

"Indeed," Looker nodded. "If he wanted, he could easily be a Gym Leader in Sinnoh."

And, Johan silently added, he might even reach the Elite Four level one day.

Drake's eyes lit up.

"If he's that strong, I have to challenge him!"

Johan noticed Cynthia subtly exhale.

He nearly laughed.

Seems like even Cynthia was getting fed up with Drake's constant challenges.

Fair enough.

After being challenged over a hundred times by the same person…

Anyone would be annoyed.

"Cha! Cha!"

Tinkaton swung her hammer, searching excitedly.

And she actually found something!

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar's eyes sparkled. It leapt out of Johan's shadow and, in a swimming motion, dove underground where Tinkaton had pointed.

Moments later, it popped back up, nodding furiously.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton called over Ninetales.

"Oowoo?"

Ninetales stretched before trotting over.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar pointed at the ground, ready to dig.

Johan noticed them but didn't stop them.

The area was abandoned—it should be fine, right?

"Oowoo!!"

Ninetales, using Dig, quickly tunneled underground.

Gengar and Tinkaton followed.

A short while later…

Ninetales burst out from another location, shaking itself clean of dirt and dust.

Tinkaton climbed out, grinning, holding a high-quality metal chunk.

The trio cheered and high-fived.

Excited by their success, Tinkaton eagerly resumed searching.

Then—

A loud, gruff voice boomed out:

"This… This hole!"

A towering, muscular man in a gray short-sleeved shirt, black cape, and holding a shovel knelt beside the hole Ninetales had dug—his face full of awe.

"I love this hole!!"

His expression was as if the hole were a work of art.

Johan and the others turned toward the voice.

Gengar and Ninetales glanced over as well.

"Gym Leader Byron?" Looker recognized the man.

It was Byron, the Gym Leader of Canalave City!

Yesterday, Anabel had tried contacting him during the Pokémon hunter operation, but he had been unreachable—likely busy digging for fossils deep underground.

"Who dug this? I must learn from them!" Byron asked, eyes shining.

"Oowoo…"

Ninetales hesitantly raised a paw.

Byron rushed forward to grab it—only for Ninetales to dodge and scurry behind Johan.

"Oowoo…"

Is this guy okay?

Johan's mouth twitched.

…No.

This was just Byron being Byron.

# 345: Sinnoh Badge Collection Side Quest? (BONUS)

Fossils?

Fossils held no appeal for Johan.

Even if they were revived into Pokémon…

Aside from Aerodactyl, the rest were just okay—unless it was the adorably ugly Arctovish.

But Aerodactyl… Johan wasn't ready to raise one yet.

…Wait.

That's not right.

He might not care about fossils…

But his student, Steven, was obsessed with them.

If he could get his hands on a fossil that Steven didn't have, knowing Steven's personality, he'd probably repay him tenfold, maybe even a hundredfold!

Johan quickly shook his head.

As a teacher, he couldn't expect anything in return from his student.

This was just about getting Steven a gift.

Giving the Steven—a man who gifts rare stones left and right—a present?

That might just be a first in the entire Pokémon world.

Besides, they were just waiting here anyway.

So, Johan accepted Byron's challenge.

Given the nature of the battle, they agreed on a 1v1 match—winner takes all!

Hearing there was a battle, Drake immediately perked up.

Gengar, on the other hand, plopped down beside Cynthia, pulled a bucket of popcorn out of its dimensional storage, and got ready to watch.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar's eyes darted mischievously as it grinned and offered some popcorn to Cynthia.

Cynthia chuckled, not hesitating to take a few kernels after patting Gengar's head.

"Aren't you battling?" she asked between bites.

Gengar shrugged and pointed toward Tinkaton, who was warming up by doing push-ups with her hammer on her back.

Obviously, it wasn't his turn.

Tinkaton was already getting hyped.

Since Byron was a Steel-type Gym Leader, that meant it was her fight.

Cynthia nodded thoughtfully.

"Popcorn!"

Drake spotted the popcorn in Gengar's hands and reached over, only for Gengar to dodge.

Drake blinked in confusion.

Gengar rolled its eyes.

"Ka-kaah~!"

If you want some, go make your own!

Then it handed Cynthia another handful before munching on some itself.

Drake suddenly felt targeted.

Looker, however, was much smarter—he simply leaned against a stone pillar, arms crossed, not even sparing the popcorn a glance.

That stuff… was dangerous.

—

"Even though this is just a friendly match, I won't hold back!"

Byron, shovel in hand, spoke with a commanding presence.

Then, he tossed out a Poké Ball.

With a flash of red light—

A Bastiodon appeared, resembling an armored dinosaur with a heavy metal shield for a face. Dust swirled as it landed.

"Bastiodon, the Shield Pokémon. A Rock/Steel-type Pokémon revived from fossils. It has high defensive capabilities. Rotom~~"

Rotom Dex popped out from beside Gengar, providing information about Bastiodon.

But for ancient Pokémon, the Pokédex's data wasn't always complete.

"Cha!!"

Steel-type!

Tinkaton's eyes sparkled.

Johan smirked.

"You're up, Tinkaton."

"Cha!"

Tinkaton grinned, hoisting her hammer and stomping onto the battlefield.

"Garrr!"

Boom!

Sensing the threat, Bastiodon slammed its massive shield-like head onto the ground, standing firm like an ancient fortress.

The battle began!

Byron's battle demeanor instantly shifted—his previous eccentricity disappeared.

"Bastiodon, Iron Defense!"

Bastiodon already had absurd defenses, boasting a 168 base Defense stat—and even its Special Defense hit 138!

Of course, its incredible defenses came at the cost of poor Attack and Speed.

To maximize its strengths, defense-based tactics were the way to go.

"Garrr!"

Bastiodon's shield glowed with metallic light, further boosting its already sky-high Defense.

But almost simultaneously, Johan's voice rang out:

"Tinkaton, Swords Dance!"

The sharp clang of steel echoed as Tinkaton swung her hammer, a dark red aura forming a blade-like pattern around her.

One boosted Attack, the other boosted Defense—it seemed like they were back to square one.

"Johan's ability to read and counter his opponents' tactics is easily among the best in the Pokémon world."

Looker couldn't help but comment.

If it were Drake, he'd probably just charge in blindly.

That might work in the short term, but over time, the one who stacked more buffs would have the advantage.

Johan, however, seemed to already know how Byron would play.

He gave precise commands without hesitation—almost as if he were predicting Byron's every move.

Drake pouted.

"If it were me, I'd just use a big Fire Blast!"

See?

This was the difference between a brawler and a strategist.

—

Meanwhile, the battle continued.

"Bastiodon, Heavy Slam!"

[Heavy Slam: Damage increases based on the weight difference between the user and the target.]

Johan raised an eyebrow.

Using Heavy Slam on Tinkaton?

To Byron, it seemed like a logical choice.

Bastiodon was massive, covered in heavy metal plating, and he had trained it to be even heavier than usual.

Tinkaton?

A small, adorable pink gremlin.

How much could she possibly weigh?

One hit, and she'd probably cry for days.

…Right?

Johan's voice was calm.

"Block it."

"Cha~()"

Tinkaton smirked, raising her hammer single-handedly as Bastiodon charged.

BOOM!!

Bastiodon's massive shield slammed into Tinkaton's hammer with a thunderous impact.

But then…

Byron's eyes widened in disbelief.

Tinkaton didn't even budge.

Instead, Bastiodon staggered backward.

Bastiodon itself looked utterly dumbfounded.

…Who was the real fortress here?!

It felt like it had just crashed into a steel wall.

"Ka-kaah~(｀)σ"

Gengar pointed at Bastiodon and laughed.

You wanna compete in weight with our Tinkaton?

That hammer alone weighed more than most Pokémon.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton grinned and charged forward.

"Wide Guard!" Byron ordered.

Bastiodon quickly raised a massive protective barrier, preparing to block the attack.

A towering earth-toned energy wall appeared in front of it.

But then—

Tinkaton's eyes flashed mischievously.

Johan smirked.

"Feint."

Tinkaton pretended to swing her hammer—only to suddenly sidestep, looping behind Bastiodon in an instant!

She struck before Bastiodon could react.

"Johan's signature move—predictive reads," Looker murmured.

"It feels like Johan understands Byron really well," Lucian added.

Cynthia tucked her hair behind her ear.

"More like—Johan understands Byron's tactics really well."

Looker realized she was right.

After all, Johan's first viral video was of him commanding Skarmory against Lance's Aerodactyl.

That battle didn't just use Spikes—it incorporated Iron Defense + Body Press tactics, much like Byron's style.

And Feint not only broke Wide Guard—it also bypassed Bastiodon's Sturdy ability.

BANG!!

"Body Press!" Byron commanded.

As expected.

"Brick Break!"

Tinkaton swung her hammer down as Bastiodon lunged.

CRASH!!

A deep crack split across Bastiodon's shield-like face.

This was Tinkaton's chance!

"Finish it! Gigaton Hammer!"

"Cha!"

Tinkaton swung her hammer—slowly at first—then suddenly accelerating with incredible force.

The sheer weight and momentum warped the air around it.

Byron's face turned serious—he sensed the danger.

But danger… was also an opportunity.

"Bastiodon, Counter!"

If Bastiodon endured the hit, it could return double the damage!

But that was only if—

BOOM!!!

A meteor-like impact cratered the battlefield.

Dust exploded into the air.

Drake swallowed.

"…That was brutal."

If Bastiodon survived, the Counter damage would be catastrophic.

But as the dust cleared…

Bastiodon lay on the ground—KO'd in one hit!

A critical strike—instantly knocked out!

Tinkaton, meanwhile, was rummaging through the rubble.

"Cha…"

Where'd that piece of metal go?!

Byron was momentarily stunned—then he grinned.

"That was an amazing hit. We lost."

He handed Johan a Shieldon Fossil… and a Mine Badge.

Johan blinked.

…Did he just unlock a Sinnoh Badge Collection Side Quest?!

# 346: The Legendary Giant—Regigigas! (BONUS)

"Mr. Byron."

Riley returned from scouting ahead and greeted Byron with a smile.

"Oh, Riley!"

Byron let out a hearty laugh.

The two were quite familiar with each other.

In fact, Byron had once invited Riley to become the Gym Leader of Oreburgh City instead of his son, Roark, who is now both the Oreburgh Gym Leader and the leader of the Ancient Pokémon Exploration Team.

However, Riley had declined, and with Roark taking up the mantle, Byron quietly relocated to Canalave City to serve as its Gym Leader.

Because of this, there was still an unresolved rift between father and son.

"The path is clear. We can head in," Riley announced.

"You're going into the Iron Ruins?"

Byron, now aware of Johan's group's intentions, grinned confidently.

"Don't worry! I dug that tunnel myself—it's perfectly safe!"

…Why did that sound so ominous?

Byron didn't accompany them.

Upon learning about what had happened in Canalave City, he decided to return there, feeling guilty about missing the incident.

With Riley leading the way, Johan and the others ventured through the dimly lit, lengthy tunnel and into the Iron Ruins.

For Johan, this was his first time inside an ancient ruin.

Naturally, he was curious.

Upon entering, Cynthia and Looker immediately began analyzing the stone tablets, carvings, and architecture—comparing them with the information they had gathered.

"According to legend…"

Cynthia studied a broken stone tablet, cross-referencing it with her notes as she softly recounted:

"A massive meteor struck the earth, bringing catastrophe to the entire continent. The impact was so great that it even affected the land's tectonic plates…"

Her eyes gleamed with excitement—

Like a scholar finally piecing together a complex puzzle…

Or a detective uncovering the truth before the climax of a mystery novel.

"In the midst of disaster, a colossal ancient giant emerged from the earth. Using the land's ice, rock, and steel—pure elemental energies—it created new ancient giants."

"With their help, the giant deity divided the continent, shaping the four regions we know today."

Looker adjusted his glasses.

"This aligns almost perfectly with Johan's previous research and theories."

Johan, meanwhile, was holding back Tinkaton to keep her from smashing things and causing a cave-in.

He added, "Or maybe the giant just punched the continent a little too hard and broke it apart."

And honestly—

Regigigas creating the Regi trio was like a kid making action figures.

It even left at least three in every region.

Looker froze.

He pushed his glasses up again, as if trying to compose himself.

Were they really tracing the footsteps of such a terrifying giant?

Would they even survive if they found it?

Cynthia continued reading:

"…A fragment of the meteor remained in Veilstone City?"

"I know about the Veilstone meteorites!" Drake raised his hand excitedly.

Finally, something he could contribute!

His hometown was Sunyshore City, and Veilstone City wasn't far from there.

Back when he was traveling, he had visited Veilstone and seen the meteorites firsthand.

"What do they look like?" Looker asked.

Drake paused, scratching his head.

"Well… they look like… rocks."

Everyone sighed.

Johan spoke up,

"The meteorites aren't directly connected to the ancient giants. They were just the catalyst for the disaster."

Meteorites were a recurring theme in the Pokémon world.

The meteor in Hoenn led to Mega Evolution.

The one in Sinnoh shattered the land.

The meteor in Unova brought the Tao Trio.

And the one in Galar brought Eternatus and Dynamax energy.

But Sinnoh's meteor seemed to be the largest.

Not only did it fracture the land and cause massive tectonic shifts—

It also prompted Regigigas to move the continents.

The geological shifts even led to the extinction of the Terapagos species, leaving only one survivor, which later formed Zero Lab and the Terastal phenomenon.

Basically—

Almost every major event in Pokémon history could be traced back to a rock falling from the sky.

As for the four meteorites in Veilstone City, their primary function now was…

Attracting Deoxys and letting it change forms.

"Johan, you study meteorites too?" Looker asked in surprise.

Johan waved it off. "I just know a little."

"A little…"

Looker was beginning to understand—

The highest level of mastery was "knowing a little."

He had to admit—Cynthia was brilliant for bringing Johan along.

It saved them so much unnecessary effort.

For instance, now they probably didn't need to bother going to Veilstone at all.

Cynthia glanced at Johan before continuing her translation:

"…When the land finally settled, the ancient giant fell into slumber. The place where it rests is—Snowpoint City."

"Snowpoint Temple!" Looker blurted out.

Cynthia frowned slightly.

They had long known about the legends surrounding Snowpoint Temple.

However, those legends contradicted the historical evidence they had uncovered.

According to Snowpoint Temple's myths—

A massive volcanic eruption once shook Snowpoint City.

Regigigas awakened from the ice, leading the Regi trio to quell the volcano.

Afterward, it returned to sleep, and the people built Snowpoint Temple in gratitude.

"…It still fits," Johan mused after a moment of thought.

"The legend in Snowpoint Temple might just be the last time Regigigas woke up—one of multiple awakenings."

"True…"

Cynthia's brows relaxed.

She murmured, "So… that means Regigigas might actually wake up again."

Johan's eyebrow twitched.

…What was that supposed to mean?

Was the Sinnoh League planning to wake it up?!

Why?!

Regigigas had always played the role of a "protector" in the myths…

But whether it was truly benevolent was another question entirely.

And forcibly interrupting its sleep?

That sounded like a terrible idea.

Snowpoint City—

Maybe even all of Sinnoh—could be in danger.

Sensing Johan's concern, Cynthia explained,

"It's because Snowpoint's dormant volcano has shown signs of activity. We need to be prepared."

…That made sense.

So that was why the Sinnoh League had tasked Professor Carolina with researching the ancient giants.

They feared that if the volcano erupted again, Regigigas would wake up.

Or rather—

The Sinnoh League needed to confirm whether Regigigas was actually inside Snowpoint Temple.

And based on Cynthia's research—

From historical records in Iron Island and ancient texts in Oreburgh Mine—

Regigigas was almost certainly sleeping beneath Snowpoint Temple.

"So in the end… we still have to go to Snowpoint City," Cynthia concluded, looking at Johan.

Her grandmother, Professor Carolina, was already there.

The two had split up—

Professor Carolina was directly investigating Snowpoint Temple,

While Cynthia was tracing history to confirm Regigigas' exact location.

But in the end—

Everything pointed to Snowpoint City.

Johan groaned.

No matter what, they were still heading there, huh?

Well…

As long as Hunter J didn't get involved, Regigigas probably wouldn't wake up.

It was just some magma activity.

The volcano probably wouldn't actually erupt.

"…Fine, fine," Johan sighed.

He pressed a hand over his heart.

Feeling the warmth of the Rainbow Feather,

He silently prayed—

Ho-Oh, protect us.

—

Sinnoh Region, Snowpoint City.

A thick blanket of snow covered the entire city.

With its northernmost location and the cold winds from Mt. Coronet,

Snowpoint City was now at its coldest time of year.

Night fell.

Snow piled up along the roadsides, cleared by city workers.

The warm glow of streetlamps and houses added a faint coziness to the freezing town.

"Johan, aren't you cold?"

Lucian shivered, rubbing his arms as he huddled closer to Drake and his Flint.

Johan looked at the two, arms wrapped tightly around themselves, and smirked.

"…Weaklings."

Drake was furious—

But he couldn't even step a meter away from Flint's flames.

Cynthia giggled behind her hand.

Johan simply said,

"This is nothing."

—

Just as they checked into their hotel—

Johan's phone rang.

"Matori?"

"We've arrived in Sinnoh! Where are you?"

# 347: Johan—The Sky Is Falling! (BONUS)

Year-end bonus?

The academy hadn't even issued its year-end rewards yet, but Team Rocket had already sent theirs?

No doubt about it—Team Rocket really knew how to take care of its people.

"Couldn't you just send it over with a Delibird?" Johan asked.

"The item is quite valuable. It must be delivered to you in person," Matori replied, sounding unusually serious.

This only made Johan more curious—what kind of year-end reward had they prepared for him?

Surely not… a Pokémon?

"Alright then, I'm in Snowpoint City right now—"

Before he could finish, Matori interrupted, sounding surprised.

"Johan, you're in Snowpoint City?"

"...What do you mean?"

From her tone—

Wait.

"...Are you in Snowpoint City?" Johan asked in return.

"Of course," Matori said as if it were obvious. "We have a few scenes to shoot that require a snowy backdrop."

...Scenes?

Johan thought about it carefully and—

There were some scenes in his script that needed a snowy setting!

Then it made sense.

Aside from Sinnoh, Hoenn didn't get much snow due to Mt. Chimney. Even when it did snow, it was usually too light for the desired effect.

Kanto and Johto did have snowfall, but their best locations…

Were in Mt. Silver.

And sending Team Rocket to shoot a film on Mt. Silver?

That was basically asking for a death wish.

Besides, according to Johan's script, the setting was in a mountain villa, and there weren't many of those in snowy mountain ranges.

So Snowpoint City was indeed the best option.

"Ahem, alright then. Send me the location, and I'll come find you tomorrow," Johan said.

It was best to avoid being seen with Team Rocket too often.

Sure, Matori and the trio's identities hadn't really been exposed, but—

With those three, you could never be too careful.

"Understood."

Shortly after, Matori sent Johan their current filming location.

—

Sinnoh, Mt. Coronet—A Magnetic Field Disturbance Zone.

A middle-aged man with short light blue hair, a black suit, and nearly invisible eyebrows stood in the snow, his expression cold and detached.

He threw out two Poké Balls, summoning a Nosepass and a Magneton.

Under the influence of Mt. Coronet's special magnetic field, both Pokémon immediately erupted with a blinding evolutionary glow.

The powerful magnetic surge caused the surrounding snowstorm to intensify, even triggering small avalanches in the mountains.

But the man remained calm, completely unaffected.

He watched as the evolution light faded, revealing a Probopass and a Magnezone.

Then, with a flick of his hand, he gave an order—

"Destroy it."

Zzzzt!

Electric waves pulsed from Probopass and Magnezone.

The powerful electromagnetic field they unleashed violently disrupted the area's natural magnetic field—shattering it completely!

RUMBLE—

The ground shook as the altered magnetic field disrupted the mountain's balance.

Deep within the earth—

A force began stirring.

Something ancient, something powerful.

But the man simply calmly retrieved his Poké Balls and pulled out his phone.

He dialed a number.

"Hello? It's me, J."

A woman's voice answered on the other end.

If Johan were here, he would have instantly recognized—

This man was Cyrus, the leader of Team Galactic!

Currently posing as a prominent Sinnoh businessman.

And the woman on the call?

The infamous Pokémon Hunter—J.

The leader of one of the largest Pokémon hunter organizations in the world!

Cyrus spoke flatly:

"I've done what I promised. The rest is up to you."

After a brief silence, Hunter J responded.

"I understand. Once I shake off the damn League enforcers, I'll capture the Pokémon you want."

Two days ago—

The Pokémon hunters Johan helped capture had leaked one of Hunter J's secret bases to the Sinnoh League.

As a result—

Hunter J had been relentlessly pursued by both the Sinnoh League and Interpol.

Though she was too skilled to be caught so easily, the attacks had significantly hurt her operations.

To escape, she had struck a deal with Cyrus—

If Team Galactic could create a distraction for the Sinnoh League, she'd capture any Pokémon Cyrus wanted.

And since Cyrus wasn't ready to reveal himself just yet—

He used another method.

For years, Team Galactic had been scouring Mt. Coronet, searching for the Spear Pillar.

During that time, Saturn, their lead scientist, had discovered this magnetic anomaly.

After numerous tests, they confirmed—

Destroying this magnetic field would destabilize Mt. Coronet, triggering environmental disasters across Sinnoh.

By creating chaos, they could divert the League's attention, allowing Hunter J's team to escape unnoticed.

After hanging up, Cyrus stood atop the mountain, looking down at Sinnoh.

Disgust flickered in his eyes.

"Consider this… a small wake-up call for this flawed world…"

—

The Next Day.

The sky was clear.

But the wind and snow from Mt. Coronet seemed stronger than before.

Sunlight glittered off the swirling snowflakes, casting a prismatic glow over Snowpoint City.

The entire city looked like it was wrapped in a dreamlike veil.

"...Another beautiful day," Johan sighed, stretching as he stood in front of the floor-to-ceiling windows.

Frost had crystallized on the glass, but inside the hotel, it was warm and cozy.

"Good work, Charcadet," Johan praised.

"Goka~~"

Charcadet grinned.

Suppressing its internal heat emissions was good training.

It tossed the last of its charcoal stash into its mouth, crunching down loudly.

Johan twitched.

Great.

It had already finished one of its two remaining charcoals.

At this rate… he'd have to stock up soon.

"Money just disappears in the blink of an eye…"

Johan sighed.

"Hopefully, Team Rocket's year-end bonus can make up for it."

Oowoo~~

Ninetales stretched lazily on the bed, scattering small ice crystals.

Not just Charcadet—

Even Ninetales was improving its control over its natural cold aura.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Wrapped in its giant spirit cloth, Gengar rolled over on the ceiling, still asleep.

Judging by how refreshed it looked—

Last night, the city's wild Pokémon must have gotten a very… "friendly" visit.

As Dragonite blinked in confusion, Johan suddenly landed a flurry of punches on its belly, feeling refreshed.

With the team awake, they ate breakfast, said goodbye to Cynthia's group, and headed out.

Following Rotom's navigation, Johan soon arrived at Team Rocket's location—

A villa on the outskirts of Snowpoint City.

From a distance—

He spotted the trio suspiciously burying something in the snow.

Yet, they hadn't noticed him approaching.

"You guys…"

At the sound of his voice, the trio jumped and whirled around.

When they saw him—

"JOHAN!! (Д｀)" ×3

"SOO-NANCE!! (＞Д＜)ゝ"

They immediately pounced, clinging to him.

Johan felt his eye twitch.

What had he just walked into?

# 348: Candice’s Request

[You have obtained a Legendary Pokémon Egg! (Warning: Watch your wallet!)]

Johan could almost see this notification flashing before his eyes.

More importantly—

Matori was looking at him with bright, expectant eyes, as if waiting for him to praise her.

Thinking about it carefully, this was indeed a gesture of goodwill from Giovanni, and Matori had probably put a lot of effort into selecting it.

And considering that this was the offspring of one of Giovanni's Pokémon…

It had to have incredible potential, right?

Not asking for too much, but if he trained it well and got it to at least half of Giovanni's level, that should be reasonable… right?

"…T-Thanks, Matori. I appreciate it."

Johan forced out a smile.

If he didn't have six million Pokédollars in savings right now, he wouldn't have dared to accept this egg.

"I'm just glad you like it, Johan," Matori said, her cheeks slightly flushed.

"Haha…" Johan let out a strained laugh.

Still—

A new Pokémon hatching soon was something to look forward to.

Just imagining a baby Pokémon breaking out of its shell filled him with a sense of warmth.

And with Charcadet still in its pre-evolved form, its Flame Body ability was perfect for hatching eggs.

"So… what kind of Pokémon is it?" Johan couldn't help but ask.

Matori playfully blinked.

"Guess."

Guess your head.

"Johan, we're about to start shooting. Do you want to oversee the filming?"

Matori suddenly remembered that today's shoot hadn't even begun yet.

If she didn't show up soon, the trio would definitely spend the entire day nagging her with their "Watermelon-Head Glasses Lady" jokes.

"You guys go ahead. I'll just watch."

With that, Matori rushed out of the room, her high heels clicking against the floor as she ran off.

Johan shook his head, then released Charcadet from its Poké Ball.

"Goka?"

Charcadet looked around in confusion.

Where was this place?

Johan didn't answer. Instead, he handed the Pokémon Egg—still in its incubator—to Charcadet and said seriously,

"Charcadet, this mission can only be entrusted to you."

The small Pokémon looked completely baffled.

But after seeing the egg in its hands and then glancing at Johan's serious expression, its own gaze grew solemn.

"Goka!"

It straightened up, nodding firmly.

This mission… I accept!

It had hatched plenty of eggs back at the breeding center.

It would make sure this little one hatched into the strongest baby Pokémon ever!

"…You don't actually have to train it that hard," Johan said, sweatdropping.

Compared to Gengar, Charcadet was definitely much more obedient.

As long as you gave it some respect, it was easy to handle.

With Charcadet holding the egg, Johan stepped outside.

The filming had already started.

Out in the snow, Pichu was acting cute at Meowth.

"Piiii-chu~!"

Meowth clutched its chest dramatically, staggering backward.

"I… I can't take it! Someone call an ambulance—meow!"

Then, just as everyone was about to be convinced by Pichu's cuteness—

Pichu's expression shifted.

It suddenly smirked, clapping its little cheeks.

"Pii~CHU!!"

A burst of electricity crackled.

"Ughhh! I hate this feeling, meow!!!"

Nearby, a Granbull—waiting for its turn—gulped nervously.

In the show, Pichu was adorable and weak-looking.

But off-camera, that thing was a menace.

Even the strongest Pokémon on set feared it.

"Cut!"

Dressed in a director's outfit, James held a megaphone and called the scene.

To be honest—

He actually looked the part.

Beside him, Jessie was already in costume, ready for her next scene.

"Scene change! Moving to the indoor set. Pichu, you did great."

"Pichuu~"

Pichu placed its hands on its hips, looking smug.

A few staff members cautiously approached—wearing specially insulated gloves from Celadon Gym—and carefully picked up the little troublemaker, all while whispering notes about the next scene.

The filming crew began moving inside.

Meanwhile—

Meowth, who had collapsed dramatically in the snow, suddenly jumped back up, brushing off the frost.

"So, James—how'd I do, meow?"

It was like that electric shock never even happened.

"Sonansu!"

The ever-reliable Wobbuffet handed Meowth a new prop.

"Not bad, Meowth," James responded, staying true to his director's duties.

It was official—

Only Meowth could handle playing Tom in a real-life Tom & Jerry situation.

Most Pokémon wouldn't survive this kind of abuse.

Johan didn't follow the crew inside.

With so many people packed into the set, there wasn't much room for him anyway.

"…Meowth deserves a raise," Johan thought.

In fact—

The Meowth Song might be worth producing soon.

With its current popularity, it could probably go viral.

Wait, how did that song go again?

"Ah-oo-ee~ Ah-oo-ee~"

As Johan hummed to himself while walking back toward the hotel, he noticed a commotion.

"What kind of Pokémon is that?"

"It's a disaster Pokémon!!"

"Quick! We have to chase it away!"

"But… it's not doing anything?"

"I wanna catch it!"

Johan followed the source of the voices and saw a group of kids gathered in a small square.

They were staring cautiously at something.

A moment later, Johan saw it too—

An Absol.

Ah, ignorant children.

Johan shook his head.

Wait—

Absol?!

A sudden chill ran down his spine.

He took a step back and looked up—

On the roof of a nearby house, the Absol stood silently, watching him.

Their eyes met.

Johan's mouth twitched.

Even Absol had arrived…

"Have you all finished your homework?!"

Before Johan could say anything, a strict voice rang out.

A girl with triple-braided hair, dressed in a Japanese school uniform, walked over with the aura of a strict teacher.

"Run!"

"It's Miss Candice! Run for your lives!"

The kids scattered immediately, as if she were scarier than Absol itself.

"…Johan?"

Candice, having just chased off the kids, finally noticed him.

The two had met once before—

During the Snow Sculpture Competition in Kalos, where they were both participants.

Candice, also known as the Diamond Dust Girl, was both a teacher at Snowpoint Pokémon Academy and the Snowpoint City Gym Leader.

Most Pokémon Trainers had side jobs—

Cynthia was a historian,

Looker managed the library,

Candice was a teacher,

Diantha was an actress.

Very few Trainers didn't have a side gig…

Like Drake, for example.

That guy was basically unemployed.

"Candice." Johan nodded.

"Did something happen here?" she asked.

Johan glanced back at the rooftop.

The Absol was gone.

Shaking his head, he replied, "Nothing, just passing through."

No need to cause panic.

Cynthia and Looker were already keeping an eye on Snowpoint City.

If something serious happened, they'd alert the League immediately.

"…Passing through?"

Candice blinked, then suddenly smirked.

"Oh! You're here to challenge my Gym, right? Planning to sweep the Lily of the Valley Conference?"

Johan frowned.

"…I never said that."

Candice waved it off.

"Same thing."

Then, her expression turned serious.

"I've been meaning to ask you something, Johan."

Back at the Ice Sculpture Contest, Johan's Alolan Vulpix had left a huge impression on her.

And after watching his battles in the Indigo League, she had been inspired.

"This isn't just a request from me as a Trainer," she continued, eyes filled with determination.

"It's also a request from me as a teacher."

"…What do you want to know?" Johan asked.

"Battle strategies. For Snow teams."

Johan stared at her.

Snow teams?

Why would she want advice on the weakest weather team?

# 350: Johan in the Stratosphere?

I think I've fixed the recent mistakes. LMK if you find any I missed. BTW, there's a competition going on between this fic and my naruto fic. The one with the most powerstones at the end of the week, get's 10 chapters. Right now, you guys have a lot of catching up to do.

EDIT: I've checked this morning and you guys are now ahead of the naruto fic. If you keep it up you get 10 chapters when the powerstones reset.

---------------

"Johan, please guide me!"

Candice stepped onto the battlefield, and her demeanor instantly shifted.

Both Snowpoint Gym and Candice herself were all about momentum.

Whether it was Pokémon battles, teaching, romance, or fashion—Candice believed in never losing in spirit.

"Hah! Great energy, I like it." Sitting in the spectator stands, Gardenia puffed up her chest in approval.

She, too, was someone who valued momentum.

Candice shot her a glance, pursed her lips, and then threw out her Poké Ball.

"Go, Abomasnow!"

The massive Abomasnow had a humanoid shape and was the inspiration behind some ancient "Yeti" legends. But its Grass/Ice typing made it one of the most fragile Pokémon defensively.

However, Abomasnow had one excellent trait—Snow Warning.

"Summon the blizzard, Abomasnow!" Candice shouted with force.

Whooosh—

The battle arena was quickly engulfed in whirling snow, and a fresh layer of frost began accumulating on the ground.

At the same time, Johan also threw out his own Poké Ball.

"Ku!!"

A burst of red light revealed Kubfu, who leapt into the arena, tightened its headband, and clenched its fists.

Its serious expression and towering aura were in no way inferior to Abomasnow's.

When it came to spirit, Kubfu—the self-proclaimed "chosen warrior"—was one of Johan's top-ranked Pokémon.

"Ku?"

Who are you? I do not battle nameless foes!

Kubfu pointed dramatically at Abomasnow.

"Ka?"

Abomasnow, who had just been fully revved up, visibly froze for a second. Its momentum instantly dropped by half.

Meanwhile, Gardenia suddenly looked enlightened. She grabbed Lucian's sleeve and whispered,

"When I battled Johan, was this how he broke my momentum?"

Lucian blinked, slowly processing it.

"…Was it?"

"Yes!"

Gardenia's eyes lit up.

She understood!

Back on the battlefield, Abomasnow scratched its head, at a loss for words.

After thinking for a moment, it asked back,

"Ka?"

Who… are you?

Kubfu raised its chin, snorted proudly, and declared with absolute confidence,

"Ku~ ku~!!"

Hear my name and tremble! I am the great General Kubfu!

Johan almost lost his composure.

Maybe he shouldn't have hyped up Three Kingdoms characters so much…

"Kubfu, focus on the battle."

"Ku!!"

Kubfu spread its arms, its aura growing even stronger.

Candice, who had been watching this interaction unfold, now wore a thoughtful expression.

So battles could be approached like this too…?

With the referee's signal, the match officially began!

"Abomasnow, use Ice Shard!"

"Ka—!"

Abomasnow shuddered, sending frigid ice crystals slicing through the air toward Kubfu.

Crack! Crack! Crack!

Almost instantly, frost began forming on Kubfu's fur, growing thicker by the second. It looked like it was about to be completely frozen over.

[Ice Shard: Ice-type move, always moves first!]

"Hah! Even Johan gets frozen sometimes!" Gardenia laughed.

But Johan remained completely unfazed.

"Kubfu."

"Ku!!"

In an instant, the ice encasing Kubfu shattered into pieces.

Candice's eyes widened.

"…How?"

Kubfu grinned, shaking off the frost from its fur.

Honestly—

Abomasnow's cold doesn't even compare to my beautiful Alolan Ninetales'!

After all, Ninetales hadn't just increased Johan's resistance to the cold—

It had also made all of his Pokémon more cold-resistant.

"Close Combat!"

Boom!!

Kubfu's eyes sharpened, and with a powerful stomp, it launched itself forward, kicking up a wave of snow behind it.

"Abomasnow, Aurora Veil!" Candice called out quickly.

Bzzzt—

Abomasnow rapidly swung its arms, summoning a shimmering veil of light.

"Ku!"

Kubfu smirked.

Not enough.

Bam! Bam! Bam!—

Kubfu threw punch after punch, breaking through the Aurora Veil like shattering glass.

After just a few strikes, the entire protective barrier had been destroyed.

But even so, Abomasnow had barely managed to stall Kubfu's attack.

Boom!!

Kubfu's final punch landed, sending Abomasnow skidding backward, carving deep trenches into the ice-covered ground.

Candice wasn't done yet. She saw an opening and immediately countered.

"Now, Ice Punch!"

Close Combat might be strong, but it also lowers the user's defense. This was the perfect time to counterattack.

And—Abomasnow was balanced in both Attack and Special Attack!

However…

"Counter!"

Before Candice's command even fully registered, Johan's counterattack had already been set in motion.

Crack!!

Abomasnow's freezing punch struck Kubfu's body—but instead of sending it flying, Kubfu stood firm.

Its whole body glowed with a crimson energy—

"Ku!!"

A devastating counterpunch erupted forward!

Boom!!

The massive Abomasnow was sent soaring in an arc before slamming hard into the battlefield.

Kubfu calmly pulled back its fist, adjusted its headband, and gave a respectful nod.

Not bad.

Johan smirked.

"Having momentum is great. But you need to distinguish between a real opening and a trap."

Candice blinked.

Lucian, surprisingly quick-witted for once, suddenly had a realization.

"Using Close Combat was a deliberate bait, right?" he muttered.

"If Candice's Pokémon took the bait and physically attacked, Johan could Counter. And since Abomasnow is a balanced Pokémon, it was bound to use physical moves at close range…"

This battle wasn't just about tactics.

Johan was controlling Candice's Pokémon's decisions.

It wasn't just about setting up a strategy—he was influencing the opponent's entire battle flow.

Lucian turned to Gardenia, who looked a bit shaken.

She had just fully realized something.

Johan wasn't just fighting in the battle.

He was playing at a level where he was predicting every move before it happened.

…He was in the stratosphere.

Lucian turned back to Candice.

With a serious nod, she retrieved Abomasnow's Poké Ball.

But instead of giving up, she pulled out another.

"We won't go down that easily! Go, Froslass!"

The snowstorm still raged on, and as Froslass emerged, it vanished into the blizzard.

[Snow Cloak Activated!]

Johan recalled Kubfu.

Ghost-type Froslass was too much of a counter for Fighting-types.

But Johan knew—Candice had prepared well for Snow teams.

Even so…

The Ice-type had too many weaknesses.

So, Johan sent out—

Charcadet!

Charcadet carefully handed the Pokémon Egg to Kubfu, then stepped forward into the battlefield.

"Ku!!"

Take care of it!

Johan smiled.

"Sunny Day!"

Charcadet blew a tiny flame into the air—

And in an instant, the blizzard broke apart—

Sunlight flooded the arena.

Lucian smirked.

"…That's checkmate."

Candice bit her lip.

She was completely out of options.
